## [AwaRes: On-Demand High-Resolution Crops Boost VLM Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16932) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present AwaRes, a spatial-on-demand framework that lets vision-language models operate on a low-resolution global view and use tool-calling to retrieve only the high-resolution crops needed for a given query. The framework is trained with cold-start supervised fine-tuning followed by multi-turn GRPO using a composite reward that balances answer correctness against explicit crop-cost penalties. VLMs typically must choose between high-resolution inputs that capture fine details at high computational cost and low-resolution inputs that are efficient but miss small text or subtle visual evidence. AwaRes offers a way to get both, which could make high-resolution VLM deployment more practical in latency- and cost-sensitive applications. Training data is constructed automatically: a judge compares low- versus high-resolution answers to label whether cropping is needed, and an oracle grounding model localizes the evidence, which is mapped to a discrete crop set to form multi-turn tool-use trajectories. The composite reward explicitly penalizes crop cost, so the model learns to crop only when it matters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models jointly interpret images and text, extending large language models with visual understanding, and are used in systems such as GPT-4V, Gemini, and Claude. Processing images at native high resolution improves accuracy but increases computational cost, while downscaling saves compute but can lose critical details like small text. Tool-calling lets AI models invoke external functions or APIs, and supervised fine-tuning (SFT) adapts a pretrained model on labeled input-output examples; GRPO is a reinforcement learning method that optimizes model outputs against a reward signal.

## [LitPivot: Dynamic Literature Retrieval and Critique for Research Ideation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LitPivot introduces the concept of 'literature-initiated pivots,' where engaging with literature prompts revisions to a developing research idea, and those revisions in turn change which literature is relevant. The system dynamically retrieves clusters of papers relevant to a selected part of an idea and proposes literature-informed critiques for revision, validated through a lab study (n=17) and an open-ended study (n=5). This addresses a real pain point in research ideation: most tools either help understand a fixed body of literature or evaluate ideas against a static set of papers, failing to support the iterative interplay between reading and refining. By dynamically linking literature review with idea refinement, LitPivot could influence the design of future academic tools and help researchers produce better-situated ideas. The system uses graph-based reasoning over a literature space and surfaces moments when a researcher can make a literature-initiated pivot. The lab study showed higher-rated ideas and stronger self-reported understanding of the literature space, while the open-ended study revealed how researchers iteratively evolve their own ideas.

Existing tools offer limited support for this interplay: literature tools help understand a fixed body of work, while ideation tools evaluate ideas against a static, pre-curated set of papers. LitPivot aims to bridge this gap by dynamically contextualizing literature as the idea evolves.

## [New Metrics Measure Structural Drift in LLM Communication Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13061) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.13061v3) proposes treating the prompt-to-response-to-next-prompt chain as the fundamental unit of analysis, introducing two label-free metrics: communication closure and normalized conditional action contribution. Across 2,171 human-to-human, 58 human-to-LLM, and 8 LLM-to-LLM dialogues, the measured contribution drops by 87–92% when a response is swapped for one from another turn, revealing directional interaction structures. Stateful LLM pipelines that assemble prompts from retrieval, memory, tools, and other agents can silently drop, compress, or misroute information while every component still reports success, and existing diagnostics that evaluate isolated prompts or task scores miss this. These metrics enable drift to be measured directly from raw operational traffic without labels, healthy reference data, or predefined rules, which could improve reliability evaluation for multi-agent and stateful LLM systems. The approach requires only raw prompts and responses, allowing drift to be defined and measured directly from operational traffic rather than inferred from eventual task failure. The paper notes that establishing prospective detection performance is the next step, meaning the metrics have not yet been validated for predicting failures before they occur.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models increasingly run in stateful pipelines where each prompt is assembled from retrieval results, memory, tools, and other agents, rather than being a single standalone input. In such pipelines, information that should shape the next response can be lost or misrouted even though each component reports success, a phenomenon known as drift. Existing evaluation methods typically score isolated prompts, responses, or final task outcomes, which cannot capture the relationship between a prompt and the response it draws.

## [ShadowMem Uses Shadow Memory to Defend LLM Agents Against Long-Horizon Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03228) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.03228v2) introduces ShadowMem, a defensive framework that maintains a dedicated, safety-focused shadow memory to distill and retain safety-critical context across an LLM agent's full execution trajectory, proactively assessing the risk of pending actions before they run. The authors report that ShadowMem substantially outperforms existing defenses across diverse long-horizon threats in detection accuracy, achieves early-stage detection for most attacks, and adds only negligible overhead to agent utility, with artifacts released at github.com/ZJUWYH/ShadowMem. As LLM agents are deployed in critical domains, adversaries increasingly exploit extended multi-turn interactions to steer behavior in ways that single-turn safeguards miss, so a defense that specifically targets long-horizon threats fills an important gap in AI safety and agent security. ShadowMem claims to be the first framework to detect and mitigate such threats using an agentic memory approach, potentially establishing a new paradigm for protecting real-world agent deployments. ShadowMem is inspired by the "shadow stack" abstraction in systems security, maintaining a parallel safety-focused memory that shadows the agent's normal execution rather than relying on single-turn filters. The paper reports strong detection accuracy and early-stage detection for most attacks with negligible utility overhead, though the abstract does not specify benchmark details, model backbones, or the exact attack families tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: A shadow stack is a security mechanism that keeps a second, separate copy of a program's call stack to protect return addresses from tampering, such as in stack buffer overflow or call stack spoofing attacks. LLM agents are AI systems that use a large language model to plan and execute multi-step tasks by interacting with tools and environments over many turns. Long-horizon threats are attacks that unfold across many turns, incrementally steering an agent toward malicious goals in ways that are improbable or invisible in a single-turn setting, and agentic memory refers to memory systems that let agents store, organize, and retrieve context across long interactions.

## [EA-WM Projects Robot Actions into Camera View for Better Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released EA-WM, an event-aware generative world model that projects low-dimensional robot actions and kinematic states directly into the target camera view as Structured Kinematic-to-Visual Action Fields (KVAFs), rather than injecting them as abstract tokens. Built on the Wan2.2 diffusion backbone, it achieves state-of-the-art performance on the WorldArena benchmark, outperforming existing baselines by a significant margin. Most world-action models treat video generation as an auxiliary representation for policy learning, so they underexplore the inverse problem of using action signals to guide video synthesis. EA-WM closes the loop between kinematic control and visual perception, which could improve physical consistency and geometric accuracy in robotic rollouts and benefit both robotics and generative modeling research. EA-WM introduces event-aware bidirectional fusion blocks that modulate cross-branch attention to capture object state changes and interaction dynamics, along with Event-Difference Latent Supervision (EDLS) that trains fusion modules to recognize regions of high temporal activity. The work is an arXiv preprint (arXiv:2605.06192v2) and has no community discussion provided.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Video diffusion models extend image diffusion architectures to generate temporally coherent video and serve as powerful spatiotemporal generative priors. Robotic world models use such priors to predict future video rollouts conditioned on actions, but aligning low-dimensional control signals with high-dimensional visual synthesis remains challenging. EA-WM addresses this by lifting actions into the camera view through robot kinematics and camera projection.

## [ProteinJEPA adds latent prediction to protein language model pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07554) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ProteinJEPA supplements standard masked language modeling (MLM) with a cosine loss that predicts the half-depth hidden states of a teacher model from the unmasked sequence. Tested on 19 tasks with ESM2 at 35M and 150M parameters across three pretraining seeds, MLM+JEPA outperformed compute-matched and step-matched MLM-only continued training in 78 and 76 of 114 comparisons, with the largest gains on structure- and homology-sensitive tasks such as SCOPe-40 retrieval (+6.1 points Recall@1) and remote homology (+2.7 points accuracy). This is the first application of Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) ideas to protein language models, showing that latent prediction can complement rather than replace masked language modeling. The consistent gains on structure- and homology-sensitive tasks suggest a practical recipe for improving protein representation learning, which underpins downstream applications like protein design and function prediction. The gains are modest (median +0.0106 on structure/homology tasks vs +0.0041 elsewhere) and increase with model size from 8M to 150M parameters; from random initialization the gain is smaller and replicates inconsistently across seeds (p=0.059). Ablations show cosine loss beats mean squared error, adding shallower targets removes most of the task gain, and JEPA-only training collapses downstream performance, confirming that latent prediction complements MLM rather than replacing it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Protein language models (pLMs) such as ESM2 are deep neural networks trained on large protein sequence databases to learn contextual representations of amino acids, typically via masked language modeling where some residues are hidden and must be predicted. Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA), popularized by Yann LeCun's I-JEPA for images, instead learns by predicting abstract latent representations of targets rather than reconstructing raw inputs, aiming to capture semantically meaningful structure while ignoring irrelevant detail. ProteinJEPA combines these two paradigms by adding a latent-prediction objective on top of MLM during continued pretraining.

## [DreamAvoid Uses Test-Time Dreaming to Prevent VLA Robot Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11750) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced DreamAvoid, a critical-phase test-time dreaming framework that helps Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models anticipate and avoid failures during fine-grained manipulation. It combines a Dream Trigger to detect critical phases, an Action Proposer to sample candidate action chunks, and a Dream Evaluator trained on success, failure, and boundary data to select optimal actions, achieving 72.5% success across four real-world tasks versus 48.8% for the base policy and 54.4% for GPC-RANK. VLA models are increasingly used as generalist robot policies but remain brittle during critical manipulation phases, where small errors cascade into irrecoverable failures. DreamAvoid addresses this by adding explicit failure awareness at test time without retraining the base policy, potentially improving reliability for real-world robotic deployment. The Dream Evaluator is jointly trained on mixed data including success, failure, and boundary cases, and the autonomous boundary learning paradigm refines the system's understanding of the subtle success-failure boundary. The approach operates at test time by sampling multiple candidate action chunks and dreaming their short-horizon futures to evaluate and select the best action.

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models combine a pretrained vision-language model with an action output, trained on robot demonstrations to translate natural language commands into control signals. Test-time adaptation adjusts a trained model during deployment to handle distribution shifts without access to original training labels.

## [TRACE: Routing-Aware Expert Calibration for MoE Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.10338v2) proposes TRACE, a Targeted Routing-Aware Calibration of Experts method for machine unlearning in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models. TRACE detects forget-critical experts from offline activation statistics and reweights token-level retain losses so each selected expert's retain-side activation frequency better matches its forget-side counterpart, yielding a 9% relative utility improvement over the strongest baseline under comparable forgetting quality. This work addresses a novel and underexplored problem—machine unlearning in MoE architectures—which is increasingly relevant as MoE models like Mixtral, DeepSeek, and Llama 4 become mainstream. By improving the forget-utility trade-off, TRACE could help make privacy compliance and AI safety interventions more practical for sparse large language models. TRACE is evaluated on the WMDP and MUSE-BOOKS benchmarks across multiple MoE LLMs, achieving the best performance on three out of four MUSE-BOOKS metrics. The core insight is that forget data disproportionately activates a small subset of experts that receive weak activation from retain data, leaving these forget-critical experts under-regularized during unlearning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training data or knowledge from a model without full retraining, which is important for privacy and compliance. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures use a router at each layer to assign each token to a sparse subset of experts, enabling efficient scaling but complicating unlearning because different data activate different experts. TRACE targets the resulting forget-retain routing mismatch by calibrating retain regularization specifically for forget-critical experts.

## [Guided-OPD Adds Curriculum Turn-Level Guidance to On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15912) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2606.15912v2) proposes Guided On-Policy Distillation (Guided-OPD), which mixes teacher- and student-generated turns within each rollout and schedules the teacher's intervention probability along a curriculum that decays to zero. On ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, and WebShop, distilling Qwen3 students from a Qwen3-30B-A3B teacher yields average relative gains of 21.1% in Score and 25.5% in Success Rate over vanilla on-policy distillation, with larger gains on smaller students. This addresses a known failure mode in on-policy distillation for multi-turn agents, where small student errors compound across turns and push trajectories outside the teacher's familiar state distribution. If validated, it could make it practical to deploy much smaller, cheaper agent models that retain the capabilities of very large teachers. The method keeps early trajectories close to the teacher distribution via strong guidance and then gradually withdraws that guidance to recover the purely on-policy regime used at inference. Results are reported on three benchmarks (ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, WebShop) with Qwen3 students distilled from a Qwen3-30B-A3B teacher, though the paper is a preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation is a knowledge distillation technique where the student model generates its own trajectories and a high-performing teacher grades each token, rather than the student imitating fixed teacher-generated data. Multi-turn agents plan, invoke tools, and interact with environments over several turns, so errors in early turns can propagate and derail later turns. Curriculum learning is a training strategy that presents examples in order of increasing difficulty, which Guided-OPD adapts by scheduling teacher intervention over turns.

## [Paper Proves Safety Verification Impossible for Self-Improving AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.28639v3) establishes mathematical limits showing that algorithmic safety verification for Turing-complete self-modifying and recursively self-improving systems is fundamentally unverifiable. It proves that no verifier can be simultaneously sound, complete, and tractable, using Rice's theorem, Gödel's theorems, Trakhtenbrot's theorem, and complexity results (coNP-complete for policy verification, PSPACE-complete for synthesis). This work provides rigorous theoretical grounding for AI safety and alignment discussions, suggesting that general algorithmic safety verification for advanced self-improving AI may be impossible. It implies that any total supervisor algorithm will err on an undecidable set of systems, challenging hopes for fully verified safe recursive self-improvement. The paper shows that one step of self-modification is never harder than the safety property itself, but persistence along the whole trajectory can be Π⁰₂-complete. Certification by a total algorithm is possible only for transformations of restricted expressivity, not merely for systems that stop changing; a monitor that raises an alarm on violation semidecides the property, and comparison against a frozen reference keeps the full theory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Rice's theorem states that all non-trivial semantic properties of programs are undecidable, generalizing the halting problem. Trakhtenbrot's theorem says validity in first-order logic over finite models is undecidable, and the s-m-n theorem (Kleene's parameter theorem) allows uniform substitution of parameters into programs. These computability and complexity results form the foundation for analyzing whether safety properties can be algorithmically verified in self-modifying systems.

## [Conditional Co-Ablation Recovers Self-Repair Backups in Transformer Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces conditional co-ablation (CoAx), a label-free, output-grounded score that ranks candidate components by how much their ablation effect grows after a primary circuit set is removed. On GPT-2-small's Indirect Object Identification (IOI) circuit, CoAx recovers documented backup heads at 0.941 ROC-AUC, versus 0.815 for the strongest intact-state attribution baseline and 0.758 for a matched conditional-energy control. Self-repair is a known blind spot in mechanistic interpretability: ablating a primary component can activate a dormant backup, making circuits that explain intact behavior incomplete under intervention. CoAx provides a principled way to identify these backup components, which is important for AI safety research that relies on causal circuit analysis to understand and verify transformer behavior. The method is label-free and output-grounded, requiring only a single-ablation baseline and one conditional pass to measure growth in Fisher-weighted ablation energy. Recovery drops to 0.40 +/- 0.13 AUC for alternative component sets matched in behavioral effect, output displacement, and depth, showing specificity to the removed circuit; CoAx-selected heads are causally load-bearing, reducing incompleteness from 0.75 to 0.21 when added to the incomplete circuit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer transformer language models into human-understandable circuits—sets of internal components that causally support a behavior. A key technique is ablation, where components are removed to test their causal role, but self-repair complicates this because the model can compensate using dormant backup components. The Indirect Object Identification (IOI) task in GPT-2-small is a well-studied benchmark circuit in this field.

## [LIFT Adds Force Reactivity to Pretrained VLA Policies via Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14236) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released LIFT (Late Reactive Injection of Force for VLA Post-Training), a framework that grafts a reactive action expert beside the original action expert of a pretrained vision-language-action policy and injects recent 6D end-effector force through causal force memory and zero-initialized cross-attention. It couples this reactive force injection with an online DAgger loop trained on a mix of offline task-alignment data and human-corrected online rollouts, with code available at github.com/y-wng/lift. Pretrained VLA policies are largely vision-driven and degrade once manipulation enters contact states where the scene is occluded, depth is ambiguous, or small force errors push execution off the offline demonstration distribution. LIFT shows that force-aware post-training can restore contact reactivity while preserving general manipulation knowledge, which could improve robustness for contact-rich tasks in robotics and embodied AI. LIFT initializes the new reactive action expert from pretrained action weights, uses zero-initialized cross-attention so the graft does not disrupt the original policy at the start, and refreshes actions during execution. Ablations across towel folding, book insertion, and Hanoi ring placement show that both reactive force memory and online corrective data are important for robust contact-rich manipulation.

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models combine a vision-language backbone with an action expert that generates robot action sequences from images and language instructions, typically through cross-attention that lets the action expert query the scene understanding. Force/torque sensing at the end-effector provides 6D wrench measurements (three forces and three torques) that are especially useful when vision is occluded during contact. DAgger is an imitation-learning technique that iteratively collects human corrections on the policy's own rollouts to address distribution shift.

The framework uses an LLM to infer application context from the database schema and then derive role responsibilities and access scopes, with domain experts performing metric-guided screening of generated roles. Evaluation metrics specifically identify RBAC failure modes, distinguishing between a model that violates access policies and one that wrongly rejects a query answerable with permitted data.

## [OptR: Output-Aware Rotation Enables INT2 KV-Cache Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02691) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2608.02691v4) introduces OptR, an output-aware rotation method that minimizes post-W_O attention-output error to enable effective INT2 KV-cache quantization. OptR decomposes the error into key- and value-induced terms, learns per-head orthogonal corrections through the full INT2 quantization and attention path, and applies an attention-equivalent key reparameterization to reduce large channel-wise offsets without changing the softmax distribution. KV-cache memory and bandwidth are major bottlenecks in long-context LLM inference, and INT2 quantization could dramatically reduce these costs, but existing rotation-based methods optimize pre-readout statistics rather than the final model output. OptR consistently improves QuaRot and OSCAR across three models and five reasoning and coding benchmarks while preserving the paged KV-cache format with negligible inference overhead, potentially making ultra-low-bit KV-cache quantization practical for real deployments. OptR targets the mismatch between optimizing cache statistics or proxy errors before the complete attention readout and the actual error propagated through attention and the output projection W_O. It learns per-head orthogonal corrections through the full INT2 quantization and attention path, and its attention-equivalent key reparameterization reduces large channel-wise offsets without altering the softmax distribution, preserving the paged KV-cache format with negligible inference overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: The key-value (KV) cache stores intermediate attention states during autoregressive LLM inference, and its size grows with context length, making it a major memory and bandwidth bottleneck for long-context tasks. Quantization reduces the numerical precision of these cached tensors to save memory, but extreme low-bit formats like INT2 suffer severe accuracy loss due to outliers in activations. Rotation-based quantization methods such as QuaRot and QuIP apply orthogonal transforms to smooth out these outliers before quantization, and OptR builds on this line of work by making the rotation aware of the final attention output error after the output projection W_O.

## [How Shared State Is Described Decides Whether LLM Agents Synchronize](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06968) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.06968v2) reports a large-scale controlled experiment in which language-model agents on a circle chose to advance, stay, or move back after reading descriptions of the other agents' relative positions, yielding 507,112 valid responses across three model families. In GPT, numerical summaries aligned every matched population at both positive couplings while histograms aligned none, whereas Claude showed the reverse at the stronger coupling, and re-describing identical states shifted action probabilities in all three families. The finding suggests that the textual representation of shared state is not a neutral implementation detail but a critical design parameter that can determine whether a population of AI agents coordinates, splits, or fails, which matters for anyone building multi-agent LLM systems. It also implies that alignment and coordination outcomes may differ systematically across model families, complicating cross-model deployment. The experiment used matched populations, controlled inputs, and three model families, and the authors found that no single directional coefficient explained the outcome, since state descriptions are part of the interaction rule that turns individual responses into collective order. Notably, even histograms carrying the same information produced different action probabilities when re-described, indicating that surface form rather than information content drives the effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Synchronization is a classic probe from the study of coupled oscillators, most famously the Kuramoto model, which shows how simple local interaction rules can produce global order such as phase locking. Language-model agents increasingly act in populations, but unlike physical oscillators they do not act on the world directly; they act on a text description of it, and that description is usually fixed in software. This paper asks whether that usually invisible choice of description changes whether the population synchronizes.

## [ART Framework Turns VLA Models into Tool-Using Robot Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Agentic Robot with Tool-use (ART), a tool-injection framework that tunes any Vision-Language-Action (VLA) model to call off-the-shelf tool modules for low-level vision, high-level affordance, and embodiment enhancement. Trained on a 30K tool-use trajectory dataset, ART reportedly achieves a 20% higher success rate than mainstream baselines on simulation and real-world tasks such as pick-and-place in the dark at novel viewpoints. By shrinking the continuous action solution space through tool-use, ART improves cross-task generalizability while reducing data dependency, which could make VLA-based robots cheaper to train and easier to deploy. This points toward a modular, agentic paradigm where new capabilities are added by plugging in tools rather than retraining a monolithic policy. ART's training regimen targets long-trajectory tool-use reasoning in challenging environments, and the 30K trajectory dataset is far smaller than those used by baseline methods. The abstract emphasizes modular tool utilization for efficient training, lightweight deployment, and scalable integration of new tools, though the truncated abstract leaves details like exact tool interfaces and baseline identities unspecified.

Traditional VLA policies output actions over a large continuous space, which makes them data-hungry and hard to generalize to new tasks or viewpoints. Tool-use, a common idea in LLM agents, lets a model call external modules instead of solving everything internally; ART applies this idea to robotics by injecting tools for perception, affordance reasoning, and embodiment control.

## [BIDETA Enables Gradient-Free Tactile Model Adaptation to Unseen Sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08673) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.08673v2) introduces BIDETA, a brain-inspired, gradient-free framework that adapts a frozen tactile encoder to unseen sensors using only a few labeled target contacts. On the SITR benchmark, using just 10% labeled target data, BIDETA raises mean Sparsh accuracy from 6.86% (frozen source classifier) to 87.09%, exceeding the strongest prior comparison by 47.22 percentage points, and achieves roughly 20x faster target-sensor adaptation than the best baseline in the SITR timing benchmark with TVL. Cross-sensor degradation is a major obstacle to deploying tactile foundation models on real robots, since each vision-based tactile sensor has device-specific sensing mechanisms. A gradient-free, data-efficient adaptation route could let roboticists reuse pretrained tactile models across new hardware without calibration data, paired observations, or iterative training, accelerating embodied intelligence and contact-rich manipulation. BIDETA combines rapid support memory, support-conditioned spectral graphs, and reliability-gated recurrence to preserve pretrained representations, repair sensor-dependent feature neighborhoods, and integrate reliable cross-query evidence. The gains generalize across datasets (SITR, TacVerse Shape, TacQuad), pretrained backbones, and tactile tasks, though the work remains a specialized arXiv preprint without community discussion.

**Background**: Vision-based tactile sensors, such as GelSight-style devices, capture high-resolution contact information by imaging the deformation of a soft elastomer with an embedded camera. Tactile foundation models pretrained on such data often degrade on unfamiliar hardware because each sensor's optics and mechanics differ. Existing cross-sensor adaptation methods typically require calibration data, paired observations, or iterative gradient-based training, which is impractical for many robot deployments.

## [VLA Policies Show Similar End-Effector Geometry When They Succeed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.21659v2) analyzes 15,000 closed-loop LIBERO rollouts from four vision-language-action policies, forming 3,600 configuration-matched policy pairs. It finds that both-success pairs have a median normalized dynamic time warping distance of 0.0120 m, versus 0.0380 m when exactly one policy succeeds, an ordering that holds across every task, every policy pair, and nine sampling and band-limited representations. This suggests that successful VLA executions converge on a shared physical geometry that task success alone does not reveal, offering a new cross-policy evaluation signal beyond binary success rates. It could influence how VLA research measures policy agreement, transfer, and robustness in robot manipulation. The ratio between success and mixed-outcome distances varies severalfold across representations, so the authors report only the direction rather than a fixed multiple; both-failure pairs are more separated but rest on thin, uneven support and are labeled exploratory. A low cross-policy distance does not imply interchangeability, since a matched baseline still reveals measurable, heterogeneous residual policy differences, and under composite visual stress policy rankings and pair composition change together.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) policies are multimodal models that combine pretrained vision-language backbones with action decoders to map visual observations and natural-language instructions into robot motor commands. LIBERO is a standardized benchmark for lifelong robot manipulation that provides simulated tasks for evaluating such policies. Dynamic time warping (DTW) is a similarity measure for sequences that may vary in timing or speed, making it suitable for comparing end-effector trajectories.

## [Semantic Scaffold: A Hierarchical Framework to Fix Summarization Evaluation Saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22603) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Semantic Scaffold, an evaluation framework that extracts a hierarchical representation of facts, questions, and entity attributes from a source text, labels each as a main point or supporting detail, and reuses this structure as a fixed reference for scoring summaries. From this representation it derives three diagnostic metrics — Fact Preservation Score (FPS), Question Preservation Score (QPS), and Entity Preservation Score (EPS) — and demonstrates that these remain informative where ROUGE and LLM-as-judge scores collapse. Summarization is deployed in countless production systems, so reliable model selection depends on trustworthy quality metrics; the observed saturation of LLM-as-judge scores and the surface-level limits of ROUGE make this a practically important problem. The framework offers interpretable diagnostics that could improve how researchers and practitioners compare summarization models, especially in multilingual and proprietary settings. The authors observe saturation across three public datasets, two proprietary datasets, and multilingual settings, and analyze four recurring failure modes of ROUGE and LLM-as-judge scores. The metrics are explicitly designed to reward preservation of essential information while penalizing detail overload, positioning them as interpretable diagnostics rather than holistic scores.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: ROUGE is a recall-oriented metric that measures n-gram overlap between a generated summary and human-written references, so it captures surface wording rather than meaning. LLM-as-judge evaluation uses a large language model to score outputs, but it is known to suffer from biases such as verbosity and self-preference, and its scores can cluster near identical values, making model ranking difficult. This paper targets that gap by building a structured, hierarchical reference from the source text itself.

## [RewardVerse Uses Rubrics to Stabilize Video Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22947) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RewardVerse introduces a rubric-based video reward framework that generates explicit evaluation criteria as an intermediate representation before producing a score, mitigating scalar drift in reward models used for reinforcement learning on video generation. The paper also proposes Rubric-Guided Policy Optimization (RGPO), a two-stage training algorithm that first warms up the scorer with self-evolving seed rubrics and then jointly optimizes the rubric generator and scorer, achieving state-of-the-art results on the 16-dimensional EvalVerse benchmark and external datasets. Unstable reward signals are a core bottleneck for applying reinforcement learning to video generation, and this work offers a principled, interpretable fix inspired by professional human annotation engineering. If the approach generalizes, it could improve the reliability of RL post-training pipelines for generative video models and reduce the need for costly, drifting scalar preference labels. The key mechanism is a dynamic rubric acting as a semantic anchor between the evaluation query and the scorer, replacing unconstrained direct scoring; RGPO trains the rubric generator to produce query-adaptive criteria while continuously aligning the scorer with human ratings. Evaluation covers both pointwise and pairwise settings on the 16-dimensional EvalVerse benchmark, though the paper is an arXiv preprint without accompanying community discussion or independent replication yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Reward models are trained to score model outputs the way humans would, returning a scalar value that reinforcement learning algorithms use to update a policy. In video generation, quality is complex and subjective, so directly mapping a video to a single number often causes 'scalar drift' — the scoring scale collapses or shifts across different prompts, making rewards unreliable. Rubric-based evaluation, long used in human annotation and recently explored as RL reward signals in text domains, breaks evaluation into explicit, instance-specific criteria rather than one opaque score.

## [Causal Test Exposes Limits of Chain-of-Thought Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23065) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.23065v2) proposes casting chain-of-thought (CoT) faithfulness as internal concept grounding, using a single shared sparse autoencoder (SAE) to encode both a direct prediction pass and a CoT pass so their latent concepts can be compared. The authors introduce three correlational alignment metrics plus a causal metric Δp, which ablates shared concepts and measures the drop in answer probability, and test across five LLMs and four datasets. The finding that causal faithfulness peaks at mid-to-late layers rather than final layers, and that causally important shared concepts are not always verbalized in the CoT, suggests that surface-level or representational correspondence alone cannot reliably assess faithfulness. This has direct implications for AI safety and interpretability research that relies on CoT as an explanation of model behavior. The correlational metrics show generally high concept alignment across models and datasets, but they only identify which concepts are shared, not how much they causally contribute; Δp fills this gap. The layer-wise causal profile varies substantially with model depth and is reshaped by model scale, and the dissociations found mean faithfulness requires causal tests rather than behavioral or attribution-based probes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought prompting asks a large language model to produce step-by-step reasoning before answering, which often improves accuracy, but prior work has shown the stated reasoning may not reflect the model's actual internal computation. Sparse autoencoders are a mechanistic interpretability technique that decomposes a model's internal activations into sparse, more interpretable features that approximate the latent concepts the model uses. This paper combines the two by using one shared SAE to compare the concepts active during direct prediction versus CoT reasoning, then ablating shared concepts to test whether they causally drive the answer.

Researchers introduce OmniEchoBench, a unified benchmark for spatial audio-visual perception and audio-vision-language navigation, spanning six tasks across 197 real-world scenes, 2,972 question-answer pairs, and 900 navigation samples with first-order ambisonics (FOA) audio from 30 environments. They also propose OmniEcho, a spatially aware omni-modal model combining an FOA spatial encoder with a pretrained semantic audio pathway, which achieves state-of-the-art results on spatial audio-visual perception and near vision-language navigation performance on sound-guided navigation. Spatial audio remains an underused modality in embodied AI, and this work provides both a standardized evaluation suite and a scalable rendering pipeline that could accelerate research into sound-guided reasoning and navigation. It highlights spatial audio as a valuable complementary signal to vision and language, while identifying fine-grained localization and distance estimation as key open challenges. The benchmark uses first-order ambisonics (FOA) audio, a four-channel spatial audio format that can be captured with readily available microphone arrays, and the rendering pipeline preserves geometric consistency among sound sources, visual observations, and agent trajectories for scalable supervision. The paper notes that while OmniEcho approaches vision-language navigation performance, fine-grained spatial localization and distance estimation remain difficult.

## [SyzHarness uses LLM agents to synthesize fuzzing harnesses for kernel bug reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SyzHarness is a new framework that combines LLM reasoning with coverage-guided fuzzing to automatically reproduce Linux kernel vulnerabilities from patches. Given a patch, an LLM agent grounded by code navigation tools synthesizes a parameterized fuzzing harness that fixes prerequisite setup logic while exposing only uncertain, bug-critical parameters to be mutated by Syzkaller, then iteratively refines it using hierarchical reachability feedback. It achieves 78% reproduction success on 100 KernelCTF cases, 73% on the SyzDirect benchmark, and 80% (40/50) on recent syzbot bugs fixed after March 2026 using only fix commits as input. Automated kernel vulnerability reproduction is a major pain point for bug triage, patch validation, and regression testing, and this hybrid LLM-plus-fuzzing approach substantially outperforms prior directed greybox fuzzing. If validated, it could significantly reduce the manual effort required by kernel security and maintenance teams to turn patches into working reproducers. The core challenge is twofold: recovering the trigger scaffold needed to reach the vulnerable state and determining the precise concrete values that actually trigger the bug. Existing directed fuzzing is ineffective at recovering the trigger scaffold, while LLM-only generation is brittle due to concrete-value discovery and runtime nondeterminism; SyzHarness addresses this by translating the synthesized harness into a Syzkaller-compatible interface and refining it with hierarchical reachability feedback.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Coverage-guided fuzzing (also known as greybox fuzzing) uses program instrumentation to trace code coverage reached by each input, helping fuzzing engines decide which inputs to mutate to maximize coverage; Syzkaller is a coverage-guided fuzzing engine focused on OS system call APIs. A fuzzing harness is the code that interfaces a fuzzer with the target, and writing it correctly is often the most important part of a fuzzing setup. Directed fuzzing steers fuzzers toward specific code locations using external inputs such as crash reports or patch data, which is the setting for bug reproduction and patch-oriented testing.

Researchers released ActiveArena-Sim, an active-perception simulator with controllable viewpoints and large-scale workspaces, along with ActiveArena-Bench, a benchmark of 35 tasks across 5 categories that require multi-round evidence acquisition and memory-based reasoning. They also introduced ActiveArena-VLA, a modular suite of 13 vision-language-action configurations for controlled studies of memory writing, memory capacity, proprioceptive state, subtask supervision, and high-level planning. Existing benchmarks struggle to evaluate how robots actively acquire and maintain information in memory, so ActiveArena fills a gap by providing a unified testbed for developing and diagnosing active perception and manipulation models. Its ID/OOD protocols and memory annotations could help the robotics and embodied AI community measure and improve generalization to unseen scenes, distractors, and backgrounds. Each of the 35 tasks is difficult to solve from passive observations alone, and the benchmark provides rich memory annotations, standardized training data, and ID/OOD protocols featuring disjoint scenes, unseen distractor configurations, and novel backgrounds. Benchmark results reveal a substantial ID-OOD gap, with uniform memory sampling, increased memory capacity under reliable write policies, proprioceptive inputs, and subtask supervision improving OOD generalization, while planner-guided memory management achieves near-best performance using only sparse memory.

## [Children's Conceptions of Trustworthy AI Chatbots Studied](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25244) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A mixed-methods study of 115 children aged 8-18 who built 119 chatbots found that younger students (ages 10-13) set significantly higher confidence levels than older students (ages 14-18), and some deliberately programmed chatbots to give wrong answers while still calling them trustworthy because the bot 'does what it was built to do.' The study identifies seven design dimensions describing what children believe makes a chatbot trustworthy. This research shows that children's trust in AI is shaped by whether the system fulfills its programmed purpose rather than by accuracy or transparency, which has direct implications for designing AI literacy curricula and child-facing AI tools. As chatbots become ubiquitous in children's lives, understanding these developmental differences is essential for fostering appropriate trust calibration. The study used a custom chatbot-building environment with adjustable traits such as confidence, transparency, formality, assertiveness, rules, and persona, and also found that children calibrated academic chatbots to be more transparent and formal than hobby chatbots. Younger students equated trust with purpose-fulfillment, while older students linked it to transparent, calibrated design.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Trust calibration refers to whether a person's level of trust in an AI system matches the system's actual capabilities and reliability, avoiding both over-trust and under-trust. AI literacy encompasses the competencies needed to critically evaluate AI technologies, understand their limitations, and use them responsibly, and it has become an important topic in education as generative AI spreads. Prior research on children and AI trust has mostly examined children as users evaluating systems built by others, rather than as designers of their own chatbots.

## [QuantWM Achieves 2-Bit KV Cache Quantization for Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26425v2) introduces QuantWM, a training-free, strictly causal 2-bit KV cache quantization framework for video generation and world models. It identifies that 2-bit quantization causes temporal flickering because small Key perturbations shift attention logits (QK^T) and the temporal-spatial tokens selected by Queries, and addresses this with quantization-sensitivity-aware clustering (QSAC) and principal-subspace attention compensation (PSAC). KV cache memory is a major deployment bottleneck for video generation and world models, so a 2-bit scheme with up to 6.20x compression and limited overhead could make long-video and interactive world-model inference far more practical. It also shows that benchmark scores like VBench can mask temporal flickering, pushing the field toward metrics that better capture temporal consistency. The paper reports that Key quantization yields smaller reconstruction errors than Value quantization yet causes much larger output degradation, which it traces to attention logit shifts. QuantWM is training-free and strictly causal, and experiments on Causal-Forcing, LingBot-World-v2, HY-World 1.5, Matrix-Game-2 and Longcat-Video show improved visual quality and temporal consistency over existing methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores the Key and Value tensors computed for previously processed tokens so that autoregressive and diffusion-based video models do not recompute them at every step, but its memory footprint grows with sequence length and becomes a deployment bottleneck. Quantization compresses these tensors to low-bit formats such as INT2 to save memory, but aggressive bit reduction can perturb the attention mechanism. Attention logits are the pre-softmax scores QK^T that determine which tokens a Query attends to, so small changes there can alter which temporal-spatial tokens are selected and cause visible flickering across video frames.

## [Bastardica: Web Tool Mixes Fonts via OpenType Ligatures and WASM Python](https://bastardica.mitpit.com/) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new web tool called Bastardica lets users mix two fonts together by abusing OpenType ligatures, generating the resulting font entirely client-side by running Python in WebAssembly. It was shared on Hacker News as a Show HN post, inspired by the joke font Times New Bastard, which renders Times New Roman with every seventh letter in a sans-serif style. While it is a joke tool with limited practical impact, it demonstrates a clever abuse of OpenType ligature substitution and shows how Pyodide-style Python-in-WASM can power fast, serverless font generation directly in the browser. It also highlights ongoing community interest in typographic pranks and creative font hacking. The tool works by defining ligatures that substitute character combinations with glyphs from a second font, effectively interleaving two typefaces; users can adjust parameters like vertical scale and offset so that metrics such as x-height and baseline align. Because it runs Python via WebAssembly on the client side, generation is fast and requires no server round-trip, though the output is intended for novelty rather than professional typography.

hackernews · MitPitt · Sep 23, 22:53 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49823738)

**Background**: OpenType fonts support ligatures, special glyphs that combine multiple characters (like 'fi' or 'ffl') into one, and these substitutions can be repurposed to swap in glyphs from a different font. Pyodide is a port of CPython to WebAssembly that lets full Python run in the browser without a server, including package installation via micropip. Times New Bastard originated from a Tumblr idea by ommanyte and was built by modifying Nimbus Roman No. 9 L and Nimbus Sans so that every seventh letter is jarringly sans serif.

## [Pre-training Metrics Predict Objective Conflict in Steerable Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26929) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper studies when objectives in multi-objective preference optimization align or conflict, using seven objective pairs from the HelpSteer and UltraFeedback datasets. It finds that two pre-training measurements predict whether objectives align or conflict for human-annotated data, but not for AI-annotated data, where response length and repetition confound reward-model scores. The findings offer practical guidance for building steerable models that serve diverse preferences without training a separate model for every trade-off, which matters for pluralistic alignment research and AI safety. They also highlight a reliability gap between human-annotated and AI-annotated preference data that could affect how reward models are trained and evaluated. For broader trade-off coverage, selecting the nearest trained model and merging model parameters both help, but neither consistently matches direct training. The confounding effects of response length and repetition in AI-annotated data suggest that reward-model scores may not reliably reflect true objective alignment in such datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Pluralistic alignment addresses the fact that people hold diverse and sometimes conflicting values, so no single aligned model can satisfy everyone; instead, models should be steerable to balance competing objectives differently. Multi-Objective Direct Preference Optimization (MODPO) is an RL-free extension of Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) that uses an objective weight to span a continuum of trade-offs between multiple alignment objectives. HelpSteer and UltraFeedback are widely used preference datasets for training and evaluating such alignment methods.

## [Policy-as-Skill Runtime Brings Auditable Governance to LLM Decision Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27087) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Policy-as-Skill (PaS), a modular runtime that packages policy, compliance, and audit functions as executable, versioned capabilities, evaluated across thirteen methods with a fixed Gemma4 backend on 600 development tasks. PaS+Audit achieved 53.8% exact accuracy, 0.346 macro-F1, 0.854 review F1, 1.000 citation precision, 0.984 policy-reference recall, and 1.000 audit completeness, while adding deterministic control raised aggregate accuracy to 61.2% in a strongly task-dependent way. As enterprises increasingly rely on LLMs for policy, compliance, and risk decisions, PaS demonstrates that governance functions such as evidence validation, review routing, version control, and auditability can be operationalized as first-class runtime capabilities rather than ad-hoc prompts. Its near-perfect citation precision and audit completeness suggest a practical path toward auditable, regulator-friendly LLM decision support, even though exact accuracy remains moderate. The evaluation is limited to a single fixed Gemma4 backend and 600 development tasks, and the moderate exact accuracy (53.8%–61.2%) indicates that deterministic rule-based control helps only selectively rather than universally. The paper therefore argues for selective, not universal, rule-based intervention in governed LLM pipelines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Policy-as-Skill builds on the emerging 'skill' abstraction in agent runtimes, where a skill is a policy-scoped, versioned unit that declares what tools and resources it may use, and the runtime resolves and traces its execution. Gemma4 is Google DeepMind's fourth-generation open-weight LLM family, released in April 2026 under Apache 2.0 with up to 256K context and multimodal support. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a common enterprise pattern that grounds LLM outputs in external documents, but it lacks the deterministic control and audit trails that compliance workflows demand.

## [Physics-Informed Neural Network Improves Sparse Atmospheric Temperature Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27290) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv study evaluates a physics-informed neural network (PINN) for short-horizon potential-temperature forecasting, constrained by a pressure-coordinate thermodynamic advection-source equation with a diabatic-source closure fitted from the preceding 12 hours and frozen before future-time training. In an Oklahoma case using hourly ERA5 reanalysis at three pressure levels, mean RMSE improvement over the strongest baseline grew from 8.1% at one hour to 23.8% at three hours, and remained 14.6–16.9% even when observation density was reduced to 5% of candidate locations. The results suggest that embedding physical constraints into neural networks can deliver growing accuracy gains at longer lead times and under severe observation sparsity, which is directly relevant to climate-aware digital twins that must operate where thermal observations are incomplete. The demonstrated transfer to an Alabama heat event and the identified terrain limitation in Montana also provide practical guidance for where such physics-constrained components can and cannot be trusted. The evaluation is a conditional hindcast at one-, two-, and three-hour lead times against persistence, local-trend, and two matched neural-network baselines, one of which receives the same future meteorological forcing as the PINN to separate the physical constraint from access to future forcing. A parallel Montana stress test, where fixed pressure levels intersected complex terrain, produced roughly 17.5% three-hour degradation, identifying a terrain-related applicability limit of the fixed vertical-coordinate formulation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are a class of models that embed physical laws, typically expressed as partial differential equations, into the training process as a regularization mechanism, which improves generalization when data are scarce. Potential temperature is the temperature an air parcel would reach if brought adiabatically to a standard reference pressure of 1000 hPa, and ERA5 is ECMWF's hourly global atmospheric reanalysis dataset widely used as ground truth for weather and climate machine learning. Climate-aware digital twins are virtual replicas of the Earth system intended to support simulation and decision-making, and they require reliable short-horizon thermal forecasts even in regions with sparse observations.

## [GUI-SD-v2 Extends On-Policy Self-Distillation to Multi-Turn GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27307) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces GUI-SD-v2, a two-stage training framework that extends on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) from GUI grounding to multi-turn GUI interaction. It first strengthens privilege-following by jointly optimizing rollouts with and without privileged guidance from the same GUI states, then selectively distills step-specific reasoning and memory guidance via a privilege-conditioned self-teacher. GUI agents that operate software through multi-turn interaction are a fast-growing research area, and this work addresses a key bottleneck—self-teachers' weak privilege-following and insufficient privileged guidance—that previously blocked OPSD from scaling beyond single-step grounding. If the reported gains hold, it could offer a more label-efficient training recipe for long-horizon computer-use agents. The method jointly optimizes rollouts with and without privileged guidance from identical GUI states, and uses a privilege-conditioned self-teacher to distill step-specific reasoning and memory signals for action decisions and retention of task-relevant information. Experiments on AndroidWorld and MobileWorld show GUI-SD-v2 compares favorably with existing OPSD baselines and consistently outperforms evaluated state-of-the-art methods in both Pass@1 and Pass@3 success rates; code and training data are promised to be released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) trains a single model to act as both student and teacher: the student sees only the problem, while the teacher is additionally conditioned on privileged information such as a correct solution, and token-level distribution matching is performed along the student's own on-policy trajectories. This dense token-level supervision has proven strong for GUI grounding, the foundational subtask of mapping natural-language instructions to on-screen coordinates for clicks and keystrokes. Extending it to multi-turn GUI agents is harder because the self-teacher must follow privileged guidance over long horizons and provide useful guidance at each step.

Emergi-PersonaOS applies this framework computationally so an agent's personality can persist and evolve in a controlled way rather than drifting arbitrarily.

## [State-Grounded Conditioning Targets Direction Drift in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27606) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces State-Grounded Conditioning (SGC), a design principle that externalizes state-dependent control into rule kernels over structured inputs via Perception, Grounding, and Interaction wrappers, and identifies a distinct failure class called direction drift. Evaluated on a 200-session in-game coaching benchmark (~1,000 assistant turns), SGC lifts turn-level grounded accuracy from 61.1%/69.8% (Prompting/PE-Agent) to 96.7% and session-level accuracy from 20.0%/26.5% to 83.5%, while cutting session-level grounding failures by ~78%. User-facing LLM agents that must track live state — game state, session history, inventory — often produce task-complete responses that point in the wrong direction, and SGC offers a concrete architectural remedy rather than a prompt-engineering patch. If the reported gains generalize, this could shift how production agent harnesses separate state control from conversational generation, affecting teams building coaching, support, and interactive assistant systems. The Perception wrapper holds mean first-token latency at 1.5s versus 6.1s for PE-Agent inside a production tool-use harness, and a cumulative ablation shows complementary incremental gains as wrappers are added. The authors caution that results inform approximate state-slice orthogonality without establishing independent per-wrapper effects, and the abstract is truncated with no broader community validation yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents typically receive a prompt, retrieve selected context, and generate a response, which works for static questions but becomes fragile when the underlying state changes between turns. Direction drift is the resulting failure mode: a response that completes the task but misaligns with the current live state. SGC addresses this by moving state-dependent control outside the model's ordinary conversational prompt into rule kernels over structured inputs, split into Perception, Grounding, and Interaction wrappers with explicit conditioning dependencies.

## [BiCFlow-MER: Conditional Flow Framework for Audio-Text Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27615) ⭐️ 6.0/10

BiCFlow-MER is a new bidirectional conditional-flow framework that reformulates audio-text multimodal emotion recognition (MER) as generative evidence transport within a structured emotion space. It disentangles emotion evidence from speaker style and lexical content, builds a conflict-aware affective condition, and transports each utterance to an explicit emotion-space endpoint via a bidirectional rectified flow, outperforming all compared methods on IEMOCAP, MELD, and the zero-shot CASE benchmark. The work offers a new paradigm that combines discriminative recognition with generative evidence modeling, potentially improving how affective computing systems handle modality-specific cues and cross-modal conflict. It could influence future MER research and applications in human-computer interaction, mental health monitoring, and conversational AI. The framework uses a bidirectional rectified flow guided by a conflict-aware affective condition, and verifies candidate emotions through adaptive prototype-cloud scoring of the transported endpoint plus backward class-to-condition consistency. Evaluation covers IEMOCAP, MELD, and the zero-shot CASE benchmark, though the abstract is truncated and full quantitative results are not yet visible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal emotion recognition (MER) aims to infer human affective states by integrating cues from multiple modalities such as audio and text. Conventional discriminative fusion compresses multimodal evidence into a single prediction, often losing modality-specific cues and conflict information, while large generative affective models embed reasoning in language decoding, making emotion evidence implicit. Conditional flow matching is a simulation-free generative modeling technique that trains continuous normalizing flows by regressing vector fields along conditional probability paths, and BiCFlow-MER adapts this idea to transport emotion evidence in a structured space.

## [SHRAV Framework Boosts Computational Lithography IoU from 0.5313 to 0.8153](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27621) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced SHRAV, an architecture-independent computational framework built around State, Hypothesis, Reason, Action, and Verify, with a state-continuation core that declares reuse boundaries and separates learned evolution from numerical quantities. In a computational lithography inverse-design demonstration, four fixed-weight design updates raised the thresholded aerial-image intersection-over-union (IoU) from 0.5313 to 0.8153 under independent scalar-pupil replay, with a maximum absolute prediction-replay difference of about 0.000824. The result suggests that reusable state continuation can deliver large inverse-design gains with very few fixed-weight updates, which could reduce the compute cost of iterative physical optimization. If the framework generalizes beyond lithography, it may interest researchers in AI for science, electromagnetic world models, and other inverse-design domains such as photonics and materials discovery. The framework is described as architecture-independent, with forward configurations evolving predictive state and reading out physical responses, while inverse-design configurations additionally generate target-directed modifications and consume evaluator feedback. The reported lithography numbers come from independent scalar-pupil replay, and the small prediction-replay discrepancy (about 0.000824) serves as a consistency diagnostic; the paper is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Computational lithography uses mathematical and algorithmic methods, including inverse lithography technology (ILT), to improve the resolution attainable in photolithography, which matters as chipmakers push toward single-digit-nanometer nodes. Inverse design flips the usual simulation direction: instead of predicting a physical response from a given structure, it searches for a structure that produces a desired response. Intersection-over-union (IoU), also called the Jaccard index, measures the overlap between a predicted region and a ground-truth region, so a jump from 0.5313 to 0.8153 indicates substantially better agreement between the designed mask's aerial image and the target pattern.

## [BERT and LLMs beat TF-IDF for Bloom classification of AI-generated questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27749) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates traditional machine learning, transformer, and large language models on the task of classifying AI-assisted educational questions by Bloom taxonomy level under out-of-distribution conditions. It reports that TF-IDF ML baselines score a Macro F1 of 0.48, BERT reaches 0.55, and LLMs reach 0.79, with text splicing and learning-objective augmentation further improving ML and BERT results. As AI-generated educational content proliferates, automated tools that reliably judge pedagogical quality become essential for teachers and platforms to filter and trust generated material. The finding that pre-trained LLMs generalize far better than classical bag-of-words models under distribution shift offers practical guidance for building scalable Bloom-level assessment pipelines. The study explores feature-engineering strategies including appending NLP metrics, adding learning objectives to the input, and text splicing, which raised Macro F1 to 0.59 for ML and 0.62 for BERT. Model retraining produced the largest gains across all models and datasets, and the abstract is truncated, so full experimental details remain limited.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Bloom's taxonomy is a widely used framework that categorizes educational objectives into cognitive levels such as Remember, Understand, Apply, Analyze, Evaluate, and Create, and it is commonly used to design curricula and assessments. Out-of-distribution generalization refers to a model's ability to maintain performance when deployed on data that differs from its training distribution, a common problem when applying classifiers to novel AI-generated questions. TF-IDF is a classical bag-of-words weighting scheme that ignores word order, while BERT and LLMs are pre-trained neural models that capture contextual meaning.

## [DT4X+ improves symbolic-regression fault diagnosis by preserving non-target classes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28087) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DT4X+, an enhanced version of the DT4X fault diagnosis algorithm that modifies training set construction and the symbolic-regression loss so that generated expressions separate target classes while preserving the coherence of non-target classes. Experiments on several benchmark dynamic-system datasets show improved robustness and diagnosis performance compared with the original DT4X. Fault diagnosis in dynamic systems benefits from combining the interpretability of model-based analytical redundancy relations with the adaptability of data-driven learning, and DT4X+ addresses a key weakness of DT4X that degraded both interpretability and performance. This matters for researchers and engineers working on hybrid model-based/data-driven diagnosis who need more reliable and interpretable decision-tree-based classifiers. The original DT4X uses symbolic regression to generate multivariate relations as split functions in a decision tree, but its loss only optimizes separation between two selected classes at each node, often fragmenting the remaining classes. DT4X+ changes the training set construction and loss so that the resulting relations become fully consistent with analytical redundancy relation (ARR) properties, leading to more informative splits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Analytical redundancy relations (ARRs) are input-output relations derived from a system model that serve as diagnosis indicators in model-based fault diagnosis. Symbolic regression is a machine learning technique that searches the space of mathematical expressions to find a model that best fits a dataset while remaining interpretable. DT4X is a recent algorithm that combines these ideas by using symbolic regression to generate ARR-like multivariate relations and embedding them as split functions in a decision tree for fault diagnosis.

## [Spec2COBOLRot: Agentic AI Pipeline Generates Realistic COBOL Corpora](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26835) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26835) proposes Spec2COBOLRot, an agentic AI pipeline that combines specification-driven generation with iterative degradation guided by patterns and complexity targets extracted from real production COBOL code. The authors evaluate the approach on three programs from distinct business domains, finding that it reliably produces syntactically valid programs and moves them toward realistic structural complexity, but that preserving business behavior is not always achieved by construction. Representative COBOL corpora reflecting real production code are rarely available, which limits rigorous benchmarking of legacy modernization tools; this pipeline aims to fill that gap by generating realistic test corpora. If successful, it could give researchers and vendors a shared benchmark for evaluating AI-driven COBOL migration and refactoring approaches. The paper defines realism as structural fidelity to production code as captured by its metrics, and it explicitly warns that targeting structural metrics independently of business logic risks producing programs whose complexity does not reflect a plausible maintenance history. Evaluation is limited to only three programs, and the authors outline a more realistic alternative for future work: generating legacy programs from scratch along a simulated development history.

**Background**: COBOL is a decades-old programming language still widely deployed in banking, insurance, and government systems, and modernizing it is a major industry effort. Benchmarking modernization tools requires realistic COBOL code, but real production corpora are usually proprietary and hard to obtain. Specification-driven code generation uses a formal, machine-readable specification as the authoritative source from which implementation is derived, while agentic AI pipelines orchestrate multiple AI steps to accomplish a complex task.

## [LWCal: Loss-Weighted Calibration for Tabular Classifiers with Noisy Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26839) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26839) introduces LWCal, a CPU-only post-hoc calibration method for tabular classifiers that down-weights calibration examples whose noisy labels are contradicted by the base model's held-out probabilities. A second variant, Gated-LWCal, adds a conservative disagreement gate and, in the main random-forest study over 432 noisy cells, reduces expected calibration error from 0.188 to 0.122 and negative log likelihood from 0.438 to 0.396 relative to the raw classifier. Most post-hoc calibration research assumes clean held-out labels, but in real deployments labels often come from weak annotators, historical decisions, heuristics, or distant supervision, so the same noise that corrupts training also corrupts calibration. LWCal addresses this overlooked failure mode without requiring clean validation labels, a noise-rate estimate, or retraining of the base classifier, making reliable probability estimates more practical for tabular models in high-stakes domains such as medicine and finance. LWCal was evaluated on nine local binary tabular tasks, six random seeds, symmetric and asymmetric label corruption, and three tree-based base learners; it achieved the lowest average calibration error, while Gated-LWCal achieved the best average proper-score tradeoff. Paired bootstrap intervals for Gated-LWCal versus raw, Platt, isotonic, and beta calibration exclude zero on ECE, Brier score, and NLL, and the artifact includes all scripts, result tables, figures, and the compiled paper.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Post-hoc calibration adjusts a trained classifier's output probabilities so they better match observed outcomes, typically using a held-out calibration set; common methods include Platt scaling, isotonic regression, and beta calibration. Label noise refers to errors or inaccuracies in class labels, arising from human annotator mistakes, unclear instructions, automated labeling, or adversarial attacks, and it is a widespread issue in supervised learning. Tabular classifiers operate on structured data with rows of samples and columns of features, a setting where tree-based methods such as random forests remain strong baselines.

## [SynerT: Leakage-Aware Multimodal Model Predicts Intraoperative AKI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26848) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces SynerT, a waveform-only hybrid temporal backbone combining a causal dilated TCN with dilated recurrent layers, plus two variants: SynerT-MM (late-fusion multimodal) and SynerT-Stack (a leakage-safe stacked ensemble). Evaluated on VitalDB under a strict leakage-aware framework using only the first 60 intraoperative minutes across 2,413 waveform-usable cases (180 AKI-positive, 7.46% prevalence), SynerT-Stack achieved the best AUROC, AUPRC, and F1-max, with cross-fitted Platt recalibration correcting calibration defects. Early intraoperative risk stratification for postoperative acute kidney injury remains difficult, and this work shows that waveform-only temporal modeling is insufficient under strict early constraints, while structured clinical context plus leakage-safe ensembling recovers discrimination and net clinical benefit. The leakage-aware evaluation methodology is a valuable contribution that could raise the bar for clinical machine learning studies using retrospective perioperative data. Among 2,413 waveform-usable cases, SynerT alone fell well below strong structured-data baselines, while SynerT-MM recovered discrimination by adding hemodynamic burden summaries and preoperative covariates, and SynerT-Stack combined cross-validated predictions from SynerT-MM with strong tabular baselines at the meta-learning stage. Decision-curve analysis confirmed the recalibrated stacked model delivered the strongest net clinical benefit across low-to-intermediate thresholds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Postoperative acute kidney injury (AKI) after major non-cardiac surgery causes substantial morbidity, but early intraoperative risk stratification is difficult. VitalDB is a high-fidelity open perioperative database of multi-parameter vital signs from thousands of noncardiac surgeries, widely used for machine-learning research. Temporal Convolutional Networks (TCNs) use causal dilated convolutions to model long time-series efficiently, and stacked ensembles with meta-learners aggregate out-of-fold predictions from diverse base models to reduce leakage and improve generalization.
