## [COPE: Continual LLM Personalization via User Embeddings and Self-Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26853) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.26853) introduces COPE (Continual Optimization with Personalized embedding and self-Evaluation), a framework that assigns learnable personalized embeddings to each user and jointly performs preference capture, self-evaluation calibration, and personalized response optimization in a single update step. Its key innovation is using self-evaluation to generate proxy rewards, enabling continual model updates even when explicit user feedback is sparse or unavailable. Most LLM personalization today relies on static post-training or context-consuming prompt engineering, neither of which adapts continuously to real users. COPE targets the realistic scenario of sparse feedback, which could make personalized assistants more practical and scalable, and it remains complementary to Retrieval-Augmented Prompting rather than replacing it. Experiments show COPE consistently outperforms strong training-free and training-based baselines under sparse feedback, and further analyses confirm reliable self-evaluation, meaningful preference patterns, stable general capabilities, and robustness under shifting preferences and alternative evaluators. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review or community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are typically trained once on a large snapshot of data and then frozen, so they cannot naturally learn from ongoing user interactions. Personalization methods generally fall into training-free approaches, such as prompt engineering or retrieval-augmented prompting, which consume valuable context, and training-based approaches, which are usually static after training. Continual learning aims to let models keep updating without costly full retraining, and user embeddings are compact vector representations of a user's preferences or history that can be injected into a model to condition its outputs.

## [SsgCaps: Open Dataset for Evaluating Sound Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26854) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers released SsgCaps, a publicly available dataset of human-engineered sound scenes, each paired with a precisely structured prompt sampled from a predefined action-based typology. It is derived from the unpublished reference dataset for Task 7 of the DCASE 2024 Challenge, but replaces private-domain audio with only public-domain samples so the dataset can be openly shared. Sound scene generation lacks standardized, openly licensed benchmarks, so an open dataset tied to a major challenge like DCASE gives the audio research community a reproducible way to compare text-to-audio and SSG algorithms. It could lower the barrier for new groups to benchmark their systems against those submitted to the 2024 challenge. The authors compared the original private/public dataset with the fully public SsgCaps version using Fréchet Audio Distance (FAD), Kernel Audio Distance (KAD), and perceptual ratings, finding only small differences. Based on this, they recommend the open version for future benchmarking of SSG algorithms.

## [Benchmarking Static Embeddings for HTTP Request Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26860) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.26860) presents HEDA, a modular pipeline that combines static embedding models — Word2Vec, FastText, and Doc2Vec — with single-class anomaly detection trained only on benign HTTP traffic. Evaluated on three heterogeneous datasets (synthetic and real), FastText embeddings produced the most consistent results, achieving high detection rates with controlled false positive rates. Rule-based web application firewalls suffer from high false positive rates and poor adaptability, so this benchmark gives security practitioners evidence on which embedding representation to choose when building unsupervised, request-level detection systems. It also highlights that embedding choice — not just the detector — significantly affects anomaly detection performance. The framework operates in a fully unsupervised setting where both embedding models and detectors are trained exclusively on benign traffic, and it is evaluated on three datasets with heterogeneous characteristics. FastText's subword character n-gram modeling appears to give it an edge in consistency, though the paper notes that detection performance varies substantially with the embedding representation chosen.

**Background**: Static embedding models such as Word2Vec, FastText, and Doc2Vec convert tokens or documents into fixed, context-independent vectors, unlike contextual models like BERT or ELMo. Single-class (one-class) anomaly detection trains only on normal data and flags deviations from that learned normal distribution, which suits security scenarios where attack samples are scarce or unknown. HTTP request anomaly detection aims to identify malicious requests — for example unusual user-agents or query strings — that evade traditional WAF rules.

## [Natural Visibility Graphs Reveal Topological Signatures of Cyber-Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26990) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study transforms 76 numerical network traffic features into Natural Visibility Graphs (NVGs) within overlapping frames of 40 observations, extracting 760 topological descriptors per frame, and uses a multi-branch CNN to classify cyber-attacks on the CSE-CIC-IDS2018 dataset, achieving 96.20% average accuracy and an MCC of 0.9566. Statistical tests show that 73.1% of attack-versus-benign comparisons remain significant after FDR correction, with 4,844 exhibiting large effect sizes, indicating class-dependent topological patterns. This work demonstrates that graph-based representations of network traffic can provide strong discriminative power for intrusion detection, potentially leading to more robust and interpretable cybersecurity systems. It also bridges complex network theory and deep learning, offering a new angle for analyzing sequential traffic data beyond traditional feature engineering. The approach uses a multi-branch CNN with stratified five-fold cross-validation, and the strongest topological differences are associated with backward-traffic and packet-length features combined with connectivity, clustering, and centrality measures. However, the study is limited to a single dataset (CSE-CIC-IDS2018) and does not compare against other graph-based or deep learning baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Natural Visibility Graphs (NVGs) convert a time series into a graph where each observation is a node, and two nodes are connected if they can 'see' each other without intermediate points blocking the line of sight. This representation captures structural patterns in sequential data and has been applied in fields like finance and physiology. The CSE-CIC-IDS2018 dataset is a benchmark for intrusion detection, containing labeled network traffic with various attack types. Multi-branch CNNs process input through parallel convolutional pathways, allowing the model to learn diverse features simultaneously.

## [Exact-Mass Linear Program Repairs Trained GNNs Post-Hoc](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27092) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces an exact-mass linear program and two learned post-hoc repairs that separate mixture-weight errors from logit-translation errors in trained node-classification GNNs. Across eight datasets, eight GNN backbones, and ten splits, mean accuracy rises from 62.6% for frozen models to 63.8% with reweighting and 65.3% with set-conditioned translation. This work offers a principled diagnostic and repair framework for GNNs without retraining, which could help practitioners improve deployed models and better understand whether errors stem from poor local aggregation or from a mispositioned decision boundary. It also highlights a fundamental limitation: label-free reweighting captures little of the oracle potential, suggesting local evidence is present but hard to select. Every reweighted prediction has an equivalent centered logit translation, but only translations in a message-induced displacement set are realizable by reweighting. A parameter-matched node-only translator reaches 64.6%, showing that translation explains most of the gain while the message set supplies a smaller additional benefit; relaxing the evidence constraint is more effective than learning within it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) for node classification typically combine information from a node's local neighborhood into a nonnegative mixture of messages, then apply a linear classifier to that mixture. Errors can arise either because the mixture weights are poor or because the resulting logits are poorly positioned for the classifier. Post-hoc repair methods aim to fix such errors after training without changing the model's learned parameters, often using optimization techniques like linear programming to find better outputs.

## [FHEAT: Learnable Fractional Diffusion for Adaptive 3D Medical Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27217) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FHEAT, a two-parameter fractional diffusion operator family derived from the discrete cosine transform (DCT) solution of a fractional heat equation, and instantiates it in FHEAT-Seg, a lightweight U-shaped network (Light-UNETR) with a Kolmogorov-Arnold mixer (KAN3D). Training drives seven of eight stage-level operators to zero diffusion, cutting FLOPs from 4.29G to 0.90G (79% drop) at 0.975M parameters while reaching Dice scores of 90.47% (left atrium), 78.79% (Pancreas-CT), and 81.90% (BraTS 2019) under a semi-supervised protocol. This reframes spectral computation allocation as a learnable property of optimization dynamics rather than a manual architectural choice, offering a principled way to prune unnecessary global mixing in 3D medical segmentation. If the identity-limit behavior generalizes, it could reduce inference cost dramatically for volumetric models without sacrificing accuracy, benefiting resource-constrained clinical deployment. The operator is governed by a fractional order alpha and diffusion strength D, with D=0 yielding exactly the identity; reparametrized by semigroup time tau = D*alpha, same-resolution instances compose exactly, so any diffusion distribution across same-resolution stages reduces to a single Sobolev-type regularizer of learned strength. The surviving operator saturates at the sharpest low-pass (alpha ~ 0.9) in the decoder layer feeding the semi-supervised attention map, and retired layers become exact identity shortcuts at inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: The fractional heat equation generalizes the classical heat equation by allowing non-integer derivatives, and its solution can be expressed in the Fourier or cosine domain as a power-law decay of frequencies, which makes it a natural tool for controlling how sharply a signal is smoothed. The discrete cosine transform (DCT) provides the discrete basis used here to derive the FHEAT operator family. Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) are an alternative neural architecture inspired by the Kolmogorov-Arnold representation theorem, where learnable activation functions replace fixed ones; KAN3D adapts this idea as a 3D mixer. Sobolev-type regularizers penalize derivatives of the learned function, encouraging smoothness, and here they emerge implicitly from composing diffusion operators.

## [Verbal Attunement Beats Mimicry in VR AI Counselor Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27246) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers developed an embodied AI counselor in VR that combines conversational AI with real-time facial-expression and posture mimicry, and tested it in a 2x2 within-subjects study with 20 participants. Results showed verbal attunement was the most reliable driver of perceived empathy, while behavioral mimicry had only a marginal relationship with perceived humanness. The findings suggest that multimodal synchrony in embodied agents is not a simple additive strategy, meaning designers of empathic conversational agents in VR cannot assume that adding nonverbal mimicry automatically improves user perceptions. This has implications for how developers prioritize verbal versus nonverbal behavior when building social AI agents for immersive environments. The study used a 2x2 within-subjects design with only 20 participants, and greater mimicry exposure showed preliminary, exploratory positive associations with empathy, positivity, and humanness, particularly among female participants. These exploratory findings are limited by the small sample size and should be treated as preliminary rather than conclusive.

However, behavioral mimicry had largely been studied outside real-time conversational AI, so it was unclear how users respond when an agent simultaneously generates contextually responsive dialogue and adapts its nonverbal behavior. A within-subjects design exposes every participant to all experimental conditions, which controls for individual differences but can introduce order effects.

## [Ruby-ASR binds Japanese ASR targets to orthographic-lexical-reading spans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27289) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Ruby-ASR refines conventional Japanese ASR supervision into a span-bound orthographic-lexical-reading sequence, so each written span is locally tied to its realized reading and both views can be recovered deterministically. It is instantiated on a Qwen3-ASR backbone with a mora-level CTC auxiliary objective, and the authors report gains in lexical-reading recovery across five Japanese benchmarks without hurting readable orthographic transcription, releasing checkpoints and inference code. Japanese ASR has long been supervised only by orthographic transcripts, so homographic forms with different lexical readings collapse to identical targets and the reading distinction cannot be reliably recovered by post-hoc text-only grapheme-to-phoneme conversion. By making reading information part of the supervision interface itself, this work could improve downstream tasks such as subtitle generation, verbatim transcription, and speech-to-text systems that need pronunciation-aware output. Unlike separate full-sentence orthographic and phonological outputs, the ruby representation locally binds each written span to its realized reading, and the target is instantiated under both subtitle-style and verbatim-style transcription conventions. The mora-level CTC branch provides auxiliary monotonic reading supervision and can optionally produce a decoder-free reading hypothesis, with ruby generation serving as the primary inference path.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: In Japanese text, ruby characters are small annotative glosses placed above or beside kanji to indicate pronunciation, a convention widely used in East Asian writing systems. Automatic speech recognition (ASR) typically maps audio to a written transcript, and CTC (connectionist temporal classification) is a common training objective that aligns input frames to output tokens monotonically without explicit alignment labels. Qwen3-ASR is an open-source multilingual ASR model series released by Alibaba's Qwen team in January 2026, supporting 52 languages and dialects, which Ruby-ASR uses as its backbone.

## [TSGPD-IR: Type-Severity Guided Disentanglement for All-in-One Infrared Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27317) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose TSGPD-IR, a type-severity guided progressive disentanglement network for all-in-one infrared restoration that factorizes restoration guidance into task-level weather semantics and region-level degradation severity. The method combines a Weather and Semantic Co-Guided Multi-Level Prompt Generation Module, a Proxy-Supervised Regional Degradation Estimator that derives severity supervision without manual annotations, and a Multi-Source Collaborative Expert Selection Strategy with a shared branch for weather-invariant thermal structures and hierarchical routing to weather-specific and severity-compatible experts. Adverse weather can create fake structural responses in infrared imagery that are entangled with genuine thermal structures, which is a serious problem for autonomous driving and remote sensing perception. By progressively separating weather-induced interference from real thermal content and enabling region-adaptive restoration, this work could reduce both residual artifacts and the over-suppression of weak but meaningful thermal signals in safety-critical applications. The framework uses a shared branch to preserve weather-invariant thermal structures while hierarchical routing selects weather-specific expert pools and severity-compatible regional experts, and the regional degradation estimator provides spatially varying priors without manual annotation. As an arXiv preprint, the work has not yet undergone peer review, and no code release or benchmark results are described in the provided abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Infrared imaging captures thermal radiation and is widely used in autonomous driving, remote sensing, and other perception tasks, but rain, fog, and snow can introduce fake structural responses that are entangled with real thermal structures. All-in-one image restoration aims to handle multiple unknown degradation types within a single unified model rather than training a separate model per degradation, and disentanglement refers to learning representations that separate distinct underlying factors such as weather artifacts versus genuine content. Existing infrared restoration methods are typically designed for a single degradation type or reconstruct directly from degradation-entangled representations, which limits their ability to distinguish intrinsic thermal structures from weather-induced responses.

## [Constraint-Driven Context Engineering for Domain-Specific AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27354) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.27354) proposes Constraint-Driven Context Engineering (CDCE), a design approach that treats domain-specific technical, regulatory, institutional, and normative constraints as first-class design drivers for AI systems. The authors validated CDCE through a comparative multiple-case study with industry and public-sector partners in educational assessment, healthcare decision support, and financial-distress prediction. As generative AI moves from generic prototypes to production deployments in regulated domains, the bottleneck is shifting from model capability to the quality, scope, and structure of context, making systematic constraint handling a practical necessity. CDCE offers a structured design methodology that could help teams in healthcare, finance, and education build AI solutions that are not only functional but also compliant and domain-appropriate. CDCE draws on software architecture design and Domain-Driven Design (DDD) to identify constraints, determine required context assets, and design representations that expose those assets to AI systems. Depending on their characteristics, constraints can guide AI behaviour, enforce permissible boundaries, or support verification of AI-generated outcomes, though the approach is presented as a preprint without peer review or community engagement metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Context engineering is an emerging practice focused on supplying AI systems with the right information—through retrieval, memory, and tools—so they produce accurate, relevant outputs, extending beyond simple prompt engineering. Domain adaptation in machine learning addresses the gap between a model's training distribution and the target environment, and CDCE can be seen as a design-level complement to these techniques, specifically targeting the constraints that govern acceptable AI behaviour in regulated settings.

## [SGCA-Net Improves Spinning Radar Place Recognition Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27394) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose SGCA-Net, a spinning FMCW radar place recognition framework that combines rotation-robust feature extraction with Spatially Gated Correlation Aggregation (SGCA). Experiments on the MulRan dataset show it consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods across urban, campus, and open-road environments, and it generalizes to the unseen HeRCULES dataset without fine-tuning. Reliable place recognition is essential for re-localization and long-term autonomous navigation, especially when cameras or LiDAR degrade under poor illumination or adverse weather. By improving robustness to heading changes and ambiguous radar regions, SGCA-Net could make radar-based loop closure and localization more dependable for self-driving vehicles. SGCA learns spatial weights to downweight unstable and ambiguous radar regions while aggregating pairwise correlations among local responses, addressing the circular shifts that heading changes introduce in polar radar representations. The abstract is truncated, so full architectural and computational details remain to be assessed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Automotive spinning FMCW radar provides dense 360-degree sensing and remains reliable under poor illumination and adverse weather, making it attractive for autonomous navigation. Place recognition uses such sensor observations to identify previously visited locations, supporting re-localization and long-term navigation. Because a spinning radar's polar representation shifts circularly as the vehicle's heading changes, conventional global aggregation can lose the relationships among radar responses that distinguish similar places.

## [QRLQ: Quantum Reinforcement Learning for Quantum Cloud Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27446) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose QRLQ, a quantum reinforcement learning framework that integrates parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) with a dueling double deep Q-network (D3QN) for cost-delay-aware task orchestration in quantum cloud environments. Simulation results show QRLQ achieves 5-11% lower mean cost than availability-based and rotation-based heuristics, reduces mean delay by 17% and 82% versus the strongest and weakest heuristic baselines, and uses 72% fewer trainable parameters than a classical DRL baseline while keeping execution fidelity within 2% of a fidelity-greedy policy. Quantum cloud platforms delivered through the quantum-as-a-service (QaaS) model face a hard tradeoff between execution cost and system performance, and this work shows that quantum reinforcement learning can outperform hand-crafted heuristics while using far fewer parameters than classical DRL. If validated beyond simulation, such compact PQC-based schedulers could make cost-delay-aware resource management practical for heterogeneous quantum backends. The framework targets the complication that uniform time-based pricing is applied across fundamentally heterogeneous quantum resources, and it uses PQCs as compact function approximators within a D3QN architecture that separately estimates state value and action advantage. The reported gains come from simulation rather than real quantum hardware, and the fidelity retention is measured relative to a fidelity-greedy policy within a 2% margin.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum cloud computing lets users access quantum processors remotely, but scheduling tasks across machines with different qubit counts, noise profiles, and queues is difficult. Parameterized quantum circuits are tunable quantum circuits used as machine learning models in hybrid quantum-classical systems, while D3QN is a deep reinforcement learning algorithm that splits Q-values into value and advantage streams and uses double Q-learning to reduce overestimation bias. This paper combines the two to explore whether quantum reinforcement learning can handle the cost-delay tradeoff in quantum cloud orchestration.

## [Passing: AI Sound Turns Monorail Footage into Endless Journey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27489) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.27489) introduces Passing, an interactive audiovisual installation that reconstructs a single continuous monorail-window recording into a spatiotemporal volume and resamples it along nonlinear trajectories to produce an endless, unstable landscape. A camera-based viewer-presence detector influences transitions between rendered video sequences, and the resulting stream is fed into SpecMaskFoley, a real-time video-to-audio model that generates a synchronized soundscape. The work demonstrates how pretrained video-to-audio models like SpecMaskFoley can be repurposed as creative collaborators rather than objective soundtrack reconstructors, distributing authorship across artist, AI, and audience. It sits at the intersection of generative multimedia art and interactive installation, showing a practical pipeline for AI-driven audiovisual experiences. The system does not aim for an objectively correct soundtrack; SpecMaskFoley acts as a speculative listener proposing a possible auditory interpretation of a world whose spatial and temporal premises have been disrupted. The installation's creative agency is split among the artist defining reconstruction rules, the AI interpreting visual flow as sound, and the audience whose embodied presence alters the audiovisual trajectory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: SpecMaskFoley is a neural Foley synthesis method that adapts a pretrained spectral masked generative transformer to generate audio semantically and temporally aligned with video, using ControlNet-based conditioning on video features and a frequency-aware temporal feature aligner. Spatiotemporal volume reconstruction treats video as a 3D (or higher-dimensional) data structure rather than a linear frame sequence, allowing spatial and temporal axes to be resampled. Interactive installations commonly use camera or depth sensors to detect viewer presence and drive real-time content changes.

## [Jev System One Model Evaluated as Low-Cost Radiology Report Factuality Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27607) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates Jev, a System One decision model, as a low-cost judge of factual agreement between AI-generated and physician-written radiology reports. The single-question configuration achieves Kendall correlations of 0.573 on RadEvalX and 0.398 on RadEvalExpert with expert error counts, outperforming an open natural language inference judge under matched decomposition and aggregation. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in clinical AI: measuring factual errors such as omissions, unsupported findings, and reversed negation in generated radiology reports. By showing that a lightweight System One model can approximate expert judgment at under three cents per hundred report pairs, it offers a practical path for scalable, low-cost evaluation in medical NLP. One support question per statement retains similar expert agreement to seven questions while using 43-45% fewer judgment input tokens, and Jev detects false negation with an AUROC of 0.977 in a controlled-error test. However, local RadMatch achieves stronger agreement on clinically significant errors in both expert datasets, and finding-count and error-scope analyses show that benchmark agreement reflects report size and error definitions as well as medical error detection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: System One models are a class of AI models that return typed, calibrated decisions rather than free text, inspired by Kahneman's fast, intuitive System 1 thinking; Jev is TypeSafe's flagship System One model. Radiology report generation by AI can produce text that looks plausible but contains factual errors, so evaluating factual agreement with physician references is essential. Kendall's tau is a nonparametric statistic measuring ordinal association between two rankings, commonly used to compare model judgments with expert error counts. Natural language inference (NLI) is an NLP task that determines whether a hypothesis is entailed by, contradicts, or is neutral to a premise, and is a common baseline for factuality judging.

## [Tiered Multi-Agent AI Framework with DLT Audit Trails for Regulated Finance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27632) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.27632v1) proposes a compliance-first architecture for AI in regulated finance that treats regulation as an orientation layer rather than a fixed ruleset. The framework uses a matrix of regulatory intent and exposure, a governed policy compiler, and a permissioned DAG with deterministic timestamping to bind evidence, decisions, and reason codes for replay and provenance checks. As financial institutions in the DACH region and across the EU adopt AI, regulators demand verifiable compliance and auditability; this framework aims to embed compliance directly into execution so auditors can verify it without sacrificing proportionality or transparency. It could influence how RegTech vendors and banks design AI governance and supervisory oversight for multi-agent systems. The architecture maps regulatory intent into concrete prohibitions, obligations, and runtime budgets, with prohibitions constraining feasibility and blocking externalisation, while obligations require artefacts meeting explicit admissibility criteria. Committee activation remains policy-driven and proportionate, and clause-level legal indexing with effective dates plus capability-based agent routing ensure portability across DACH and the wider EU.

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) are computational systems composed of multiple interacting intelligent agents that can solve problems difficult for a single monolithic system, and with large language models they have become a growing research area. Distributed ledger technology (DLT) provides a shared, tamper-evident record that auditors can treat as a universal bookkeeping service and a perfect audit trail. The DACH region refers to the German-speaking countries of Germany (D), Austria (A), and Switzerland (CH), which share regulatory and linguistic contexts relevant to financial compliance.

## [LoReST Models Local Heterogeneity and Cross-Region Context for Traffic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27637) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces LoReST, a Local-Region Spatial Temporal network that models spatial dependencies at two complementary granularities: node neighborhoods and road network regions. On four datasets of the LargeST benchmark, LoReST achieves average relative reductions of 4.78% in MAE, 3.60% in RMSE, and 5.75% in MAPE. Large-scale traffic forecasting is central to intelligent transportation systems, and LoReST's two-granularity design offers a way to capture fine-grained local heterogeneity while still acquiring long-range context efficiently. If the reported gains hold, the approach could improve prediction accuracy for city-scale road networks without the prohibitive cost of all-pairs node interactions. LoReST uses relation-aware local aggregation with road- and direction-specific feature transformations to capture heterogeneous dependencies within geographic neighborhoods, and constructs region representations via mean pooling, exchanges long-range context through inter-region attention, and broadcasts it back to nodes. The paper reports results on four LargeST benchmark datasets, though it remains a specialized preprint without community discussion or peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic forecasting models typically combine graph neural networks (GNNs) with temporal modules to capture both spatial dependencies between road sensors and dynamic traffic patterns over time. In large-scale road networks, neighboring nodes can behave very differently depending on road identity and travel direction, while modeling all-pairs interactions for global context is computationally expensive. LoReST addresses this tension by separating local relation-aware aggregation from region-level attention, an approach related to broader efforts in spatio-temporal GNNs such as STGCN and DCRNN.

## [New Framework Evaluates Small Language Models on KG Reasoning Paths, Not Just Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27669) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.27669) proposes a controlled evaluation method that isolates small language models' ability to navigate knowledge graphs by using the THESEUS navigation and traceability framework with frozen, off-the-shelf SLMs as local action policies. At each hop the environment exposes legal outgoing graph actions, and the model selects one executable action and decides whether to stop, without task-specific parameter updates, model-controlled beam search, or free-form answer generation. End-to-end KGQA conflates graph access, search, navigation, reasoning, and answer generation, making it hard to attribute failures to navigation specifically. By separating navigation from other pipeline stages, this work enables more precise diagnosis of where small language models succeed or fail, which matters as SLMs are increasingly paired with knowledge graphs in resource-constrained deployments. The study evaluates terminal-answer accuracy with Hits@1 alongside path fidelity using Path Edit Distance (PED) as the primary trajectory metric, across the Kinship and MQuAKE-ST KGQA datasets. Results show that similarly sized local models differ substantially in both answer accuracy and path fidelity, with the two metrics sometimes favoring different models, and a single demonstrated trajectory can improve or degrade navigation depending on the model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph question answering (KGQA) is the task of answering natural language questions using structured information stored in a knowledge graph, typically requiring entity linking, relation linking, and multi-hop reasoning. Small language models (SLMs) are compact models increasingly used alongside KGs, but standard end-to-end evaluation only checks whether the final answer is correct, not whether the model followed a valid reasoning path. THESEUS is a framework that reframes multi-hop KGQA as question-conditioned graph navigation, making each hop traceable so that reasoning steps can be inspected rather than hidden inside a black-box pipeline.

## [Two-Axis Drift Detection Fixes Blind Spots in Exchange and Novelty Scores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27865) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.27865) shows that cross-evaluated heads on a frozen shared encoder inherit the extrapolation confound of shallow exchange scores: pure input rotations with fixed labels inflate a deep exchange score from about 0 to 0.80, while representation-novelty scores stay flat under label permutations that completely change the task. The authors resolve both blind spots by transplanting a conditional two-discriminator discrepancy into the embedding space, yielding a functional axis that stays within ±0.001 under rotations and tracks label-permutation drift mass monotonically. Drift detection is central to continual learning and open-world deployment, where models must decide whether incoming data reflects a genuinely new task or merely a superficial shift. This two-axis gate achieves better decision quality with fewer heads than exchange or novelty triggers at a matched training budget, and separates semantic novelty from photometric shift with AUROC 0.98–0.99 on generalized category discovery, where per-input OOD scores such as MSP, Energy, Mahalanobis, and KNN sit near chance. All findings replicate across frozen ImageNet-21k ViT-B/16 and self-supervised DINOv2 backbones on CIFAR-100, and extend to residual adapter pools with recurrence, where a null-calibrated novelty trigger never fires on mechanism changes while the two-axis gate handles them with full recurrence reuse. The paper explicitly states the common-factoring condition under which embedding-space conclusions transfer back to the original mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: In continual learning, models often share a deep encoder while maintaining separate task heads, and drift detection scores are used to decide when a new head is needed. Exchange scores compare tasks by cross-evaluating heads, while novelty scores measure how different a representation is from previously seen ones; both are known to be confounded by superficial input changes or by label permutations. Generalized category discovery is a benchmark setting where unlabeled data may belong to either known or novel classes, making it a natural testbed for distinguishing real semantic novelty from mere photometric shift.

## [Shared Encoders Don't Fix Task-Comparison Confounds, Paper Argues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27866) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.27866) demonstrates that sharing a deep encoder does not eliminate the central confound of task-comparison scores: cross-evaluated heads on a frozen shared representation inherit the extrapolation confound of shallow exchange scores, with pure input rotations inflating a deep exchange score from about 0 to 0.80 while representation-novelty scores stay flat under label permutations. The authors propose transplanting a conditional two-discriminator discrepancy into the embedding space, which keeps the functional axis within ±0.001 under rotations and tracks label-permutation drift mass monotonically. This work matters because mixture-of-experts and multi-head architectures are widely assumed to sidestep task-comparison confounds simply by sharing a backbone, and the paper shows that assumption is false. The proposed two-axis gate achieves better decision quality with fewer heads at a matched training budget, and on generalized category discovery it separates semantic novelty from photometric shift with AUROC 0.98–0.99 where standard per-input OOD scores (MSP, Energy, Mahalanobis, KNN) sit near chance. The findings replicate across frozen ImageNet-21k ViT-B/16 and self-supervised DINOv2 backbones on CIFAR-100, and extend to residual adapter pools with recurrence, where a null-calibrated novelty trigger never fires on mechanism changes while the two-axis gate handles them with full recurrence reuse. The authors explicitly state the common-factoring condition under which embedding-space conclusions transfer back to the original mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-head and mixture-of-experts models, a shared encoder (backbone) extracts features while task-specific heads make predictions, and researchers often compare tasks by exchanging or cross-evaluating heads. Exchange scores and representation-novelty scores are used to judge whether a head is genuinely specialized or merely exploiting superficial input differences, but both can be confounded by transformations that change inputs without changing the underlying task. Generalized category discovery (GCD) is an image-recognition setting where unlabelled images may belong to known or novel classes, making robust novelty detection essential.

## [TopoGS Brings Octree Topology Awareness to 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27868) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TopoGS introduces a topology-aware anchor feature aggregation framework for octree-based 3D Gaussian Splatting, with two lightweight components: Hierarchical Anchor Coupling, which fuses per-level context triplets via a residual MLP to create bidirectional cross-level gradient pathways, and Structure-Aware Containment Aggregation, which uses octree containment and hash-based matching to separate anchors with valid parent-child relations from isolated ones and applies soft weighting for topological sparsity. Across ten scenes from Mill19, UrbanScene3D, Tanks & Temples, MatrixCity, and WHU, it reports average PSNR gains of 2.13, 1.78, and 0.29 dB over the strongest baseline on aerial, ground-level, and synthetic-cartographic scenes respectively, while rendering faster and using less memory. Large-scale 3D Gaussian Splatting increasingly relies on octree hierarchies for level-of-detail rendering, but most methods optimize features at each level independently, wasting the structural information encoded in the tree. By explicitly exploiting parent-child topology during feature learning, TopoGS shows that consistent quality gains and efficiency improvements are achievable without architectural overhaul, which could influence how future anchor-based and LOD-based 3DGS systems are designed. The authors observe that uniform cross-level aggregation has asymmetric effects: fine-level anchors benefit from coarse context, while coarse-level anchors need selective information from descendants, which motivates the bidirectional coupling design. The two components are described as lightweight, and the code is released at https://github.com/WZ-CS/TopoGS, though the evaluation is limited to ten scenes and the reported gains on synthetic-cartographic data are modest at 0.29 dB.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a rasterization-based technique for real-time photorealistic novel-view rendering, introduced by an Inria group in 2023, which represents scenes as many 3D Gaussians rather than neural radiance fields. To handle large scenes efficiently, octree-based variants organize Gaussians or their anchor points into a multi-level hierarchy, where an octree is a tree data structure in which each internal node has exactly eight children, recursively subdividing 3D space into octants. This hierarchy enables level-of-detail rendering, but the features attached to anchors at different levels are usually optimized in isolation, leaving the tree's topology underused.

The paper introduces CASPER, a chain-of-thought attribute-specific prompting framework that generates summaries of interrogator-witness dialogues through iterative role-based evaluation, alongside MINDSum, a new dataset of 6,000 annotated utterance pairs extending the MIND corpus. CASPER employs RoleEval, a hierarchical evaluation mechanism where roles such as officer, inspector, and senior inspector iteratively assess summaries, and it outperforms standard summarization models on ROUGE and BERTScore metrics. This work advances dialogue summarization in high-stakes forensic and investigative settings, where factual accuracy and attribute-specific relevance are critical, and could enable AI-driven forensic intelligence tools. It also contributes a new dataset and evaluation framework that may spur further research in controlled summarization for sensitive domains. MINDSum contains 6,000 utterance pairs annotated with event details, factual statements, character descriptions, and fillers, and CASPER integrates entity extraction with structured feedback loops to improve factual consistency and contextual completeness. The framework was tested using the Llama 3.1 (8B) model in a zero-shot setting for attribute-specific summarization, and human evaluation confirmed alignment with expert reasoning.

## [Curriculum Learning Boosts GNN Reinforcement Learning for Job Shop Scheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28085) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.28085) studies curriculum learning for graph neural network-based reinforcement learning in job shop scheduling, comparing progressive training from smaller to larger instances against single-size training at target sizes of 20x20, 25x25, and 30x30. At the 30x30 target, curriculum learning reduced the mean optimality gap across all evaluation sizes by about 8.1 percentage points, cut the target-size gap by about 8.6 percentage points, and saved roughly 50 hours of training time. Job shop scheduling is a classic NP-hard combinatorial optimization problem with wide industrial relevance, and this work shows that curriculum learning can make GNN-based reinforcement learning policies both cheaper to train and better at generalizing across instance sizes. This matters for practitioners who need scheduling policies that scale beyond the small instances they can afford to train on. Models were evaluated on unseen instances ranging from 8x8 to 30x30 using the optimality gap, measuring both generalization across all sizes and specialization on the target size. The benefits of curriculum learning grew with target size, with the largest gains at 30x30, though the study is limited to three target sizes and a single scheduling problem formulation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: The job shop scheduling problem asks how to sequence operations of multiple jobs on multiple machines, each with a fixed processing order, to minimize the total completion time (makespan); it is a well-known combinatorial optimization problem in computer science and operations research. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are neural networks designed for graph-structured inputs that use message passing between nodes, making them a natural fit for representing scheduling instances as disjunctive graphs. Curriculum learning is a training strategy that presents examples of increasing difficulty, intended to speed up training or reach better optima, and here it is applied by training first on small scheduling instances before adapting to larger ones.

A new arXiv paper (2609.28110) proposes a three-stage framework for extending the field of view (FOV) in dental cone-beam CT (CBCT) from truncated projections. The pipeline combines an implicit neural representation (INR) to estimate missing projection data, an iterative reconstruction step for anatomical consistency, and a fast diffusion model for image enhancement, with code publicly released on GitHub. Truncated FOV is a persistent limitation of dental CBCT systems, causing artifacts and preventing visualization of anatomy outside the scanned region. By unifying continuous representations, physics-based reconstruction, and generative refinement, this approach could improve diagnostic imaging quality and reduce the need for larger detectors or additional scans. The framework operates on truncated projection data rather than full scans, using the INR to fill in missing projections before iterative reconstruction and diffusion-based enhancement. Experimental results show reduced truncation artifacts and improved reconstruction of structures extending beyond the original FOV, though the paper is a specialized medical imaging application with no community discussion yet.

## [Gradient-Based Attribution Scales Attention Head Analysis in Context-Aware MT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28117) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a gradient-based head attribution strategy that backpropagates Token-level Max-Margin loss to attention maps, enabling large-scale causal analysis of attention heads suitable for LLMs. It evaluates the method on disambiguation in context-aware machine translation across 50 phenomena, 4 models, and 4 language directions, revealing general-purpose attention heads and redundancy in head functions. This work provides a scalable causal method for interpreting attention heads in large language models, which could improve model transparency and guide more efficient architecture design. The finding of redundant head functions suggests that current models may be over-parameterized in attention, with implications for pruning and efficiency. The method aligns with the effects of increasing attention scores of token-to-token relations on three models and two language directions, ensuring robustness. It also finds that the average attention a head assigns to a relation does not necessarily correlate with model performance, indicating redundancy developed during training.

## [Agile-V Assurance Spine: Gating Agentic Engineering Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28216) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2609.28216) proposes the Agile-V Assurance Spine, a cross-domain transition contract for software, firmware, and PCB engineering that admits evidence only when it comes from an authoritative source profile, is bound to the exact artifact and frozen policy baseline, remains current with respect to declared dependencies, and meets risk-appropriate independence and authority requirements. Gate decisions are recorded as receipts, approvals and exceptions are exact-scope and time-bounded, and authorization is rechecked at the effect boundary before merge, deployment, flashing, release, or fabrication. As agentic systems increasingly edit repositories, run tests, build firmware, and prepare deployable or manufacturable artifacts, the assurance question shifts from whether an agent can produce output to whether an engineering lifecycle is justified in acting on claims about that output. This framework targets AI safety and software engineering practitioners by offering a precise vocabulary and compositional architecture for evidence-gated lifecycle control, though it remains a preprint without demonstrated production superiority or regulatory conformity. The paper positions its model relative to evidence-gated lifecycle control, continuous assurance, runtime admission, provenance, and AI/ML inventories through a bounded review of contemporary research, commercial platforms, open-source infrastructure, and standards. It contributes domain profiles, mapping to open-source implementations, and an adversarial evaluation agenda, but explicitly does not claim regulatory conformity or demonstrated production superiority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic engineering systems are AI-driven tools that can autonomously modify code repositories, execute tools and tests, build firmware, and synthesize hardware schematics. Existing products and standards already provide sandboxes, approvals, hooks, traces, policy enforcement, attestations, bills of materials, and assurance representations, but these capabilities remain fragmented across tools and domains. The Agile-V Assurance Spine aims to unify these fragmented capabilities into a single cross-domain transition contract that governs when an agent's output may be acted upon.

## [Information-Theoretic Bound on Context in Shared-State Cognitive Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16716) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.16716v4) derives an information-theoretic bound showing that, in shared-state cognitive models, residual context dependence in behavior lower-bounds both the context information and the conditional entropy that an auxiliary context-mediating variable must carry, formalized as I(C;O|S) ≤ I(C;M|S) ≤ H(M|S). The paper also provides a worked recognition-memory example computing this quantity for payoff-induced criterion shifts. This gives cognitive modelers and designers of artificial agents a principled way to quantify the context-memory-control trade-offs that arise when an agent must maintain coherent behavior across changing contexts under bounded internal representations. It reframes context sensitivity as an information budget problem rather than a matter of state-space size. The bound is representation-relative rather than a universal contextuality measure or a measure of state-space size, and it assumes the conditional independence O ⊥ C | (S, M). Ontological contextuality and quantum probability are treated as a separate specialization rather than prerequisites for the cognitive interpretation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Information theory, as conceived by Claude Shannon, studies the processing and utilization of information within a probabilistic context, where information can be thought of as the resolution of uncertainty. Conditional entropy quantifies the amount of information needed to describe the outcome of a random variable given that the value of another random variable is known. Shared-state cognitive models assume an internal or latent state S that mediates behavior, and context-sensitive behavior can be modeled either by enriching S, by letting the response rule access context C directly, or by preserving S while adding an auxiliary criterion or control variable M — the third architecture that this paper isolates.

## [PBRC Protocol Prevents Conformity-Driven Belief Cascades in Multi-Agent AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15558) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2604.15558v2) introduces PBRC (Preregistered Belief Revision Contracts), a protocol-level mechanism that strictly separates open communication from admissible epistemic change in deliberative multi-agent systems. The paper proves that under evidential contracts with conservative fallback, social-only rounds cannot increase confidence or generate purely conformity-driven wrong-but-sure cascades, and it provides simulations illustrating cascade suppression and auditability. This work addresses a critical AI safety concern: multi-agent systems can converge to false conclusions with high confidence when agents treat agreement, prestige, or majority size as evidence. By making every substantive belief change enforceable and auditable, PBRC offers a formal foundation for building more reliable and accountable multi-agent deliberation systems. The protocol requires that any non-fallback belief revision cite a preregistered first-order evidence trigger and provide a nonempty witness set of externally validated evidence tokens. The paper also introduces a companion contractual dynamic doxastic logic to specify trace invariants, and proves that for token-invariant contracts, enforced trajectories depend only on token-exposure traces, with tight diameter bounds for universal evidence closure under flooding dissemination.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent AI systems, agents exchange messages and update their beliefs over time, which is intended to improve collective performance. However, this interaction can produce conformity effects where social cues like agreement or majority size are mistakenly treated as evidence, leading to high-confidence convergence on false conclusions. Belief revision is the process by which an agent updates its beliefs in light of new information, and epistemic protocols are rules governing how such updates should occur. PBRC builds on these concepts by adding a contract layer that preregisters what counts as admissible evidence and revision.

## [TOPS: First-Principles Visual Token Pruning for Efficient MLLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27161) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.27161v2) introduces TOPS, a training-free and model-agnostic visual token pruning module that formulates pruning as constructing Token Optimal Preservation Sets based on three information-theoretic principles: Task Relevance, Information Coverage, and Semantic Diversity. Experiments across 7 MLLM backbones and 14 benchmarks show TOPS outperforms prior methods, and on LLaVA-NeXT it removes 77.8% of visual tokens while preserving 100.0% and 100.6% performance on the 7B and 13B models. Visual tokens dominate the computational cost of multimodal LLM inference, so a principled pruning method that works across models without retraining could significantly reduce latency and memory for real-world MLLM deployment. The finding that pruning redundant tokens can even mitigate hallucination suggests a path toward lighter, more reliable multimodal models. TOPS is training-free and model-agnostic, meaning it can be plugged into existing MLLMs without fine-tuning, and it is grounded in a top-down information-theoretic analysis rather than empirical heuristics. The reported 100.6% performance on the 13B LLaVA-NeXT model indicates that pruning can occasionally improve results, though the paper's claims are based on specific benchmarks and backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) process images by converting them into hundreds or thousands of visual tokens, which are then fed alongside text into a transformer-based language model. Because self-attention scales poorly with sequence length, these visual tokens create a major inference bottleneck in terms of computation and KV-cache memory. Visual token pruning aims to discard redundant or unimportant tokens before or during inference, but existing criteria—such as attention scores or diversity measures—often retain redundancy or ignore the user's instruction. TOPS addresses this by deriving three principles from information theory to guide which tokens to keep.

## [TACT Framework Post-Trains LLMs for Adaptive ESL Tutoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03952) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce TACT (Taxonomy-Aligned Conversational Tutor), a human-grounded framework that post-trains Qwen3.5-4B using supervised fine-tuning followed by taxonomy-aligned Group Relative Policy Optimization to produce TACTutor. The work also releases TACTCorpus, which enriches 260 authentic teacher-student conversations with 32,379 annotations, plus TACTBench, a 78-context diagnostic benchmark, and reports a 20.30% improvement over the backbone and top ratings in a blinded study with 50 learners. This matters because most LLM-based tutoring systems optimize for fluent, human-like responses rather than pedagogically appropriate actions, so a taxonomy-grounded training and evaluation pipeline could shift how educational LLM tutors are built and judged. The open release of data, benchmark, and model weights gives the AI-in-education community a reusable foundation for developing and comparing adaptive ESL tutors. The framework rests on two complementary taxonomies: a Tutor-Strategy Taxonomy with 13 tutor response strategies and a Student-Move Taxonomy that characterizes learner behavior by move type and status. TACTutor is optimized for scaffolding quality rather than reference imitation alone, and it reportedly maintains backbone performance on established external educational benchmarks while outperforming proprietary baselines under the same protocol.

## [HANIA: Planner-Guided Multimodal Graph Evidence Selection for Grounded QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29088) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.29088v2) introduces HANIA, a planner-guided multimodal graph framework that uses a frozen vision-language model to extract question-relevant visual evidence with explicit abstention, builds an input-grounded multimodal graph, and applies a two-group finite-state planner to coordinate descriptive and relational evidence. Coverage-aware pruning then retains a compact evidence set based on relevance, graph confidence, concept coverage, and modality diversity, which is passed to a frozen instruction-tuned decoder; code is released at github.com/Zafar-southeast/HANIA. Multimodal question answering is often degraded by noisy, redundant, or weakly grounded evidence, and this work shows that structured evidence planning plus compact graph-guided retrieval can achieve competitive accuracy on ScienceQA without target-dataset fine-tuning or iterative retrieval. It points toward more efficient, evidence-grounded multimodal reasoning pipelines that could reduce unsupported generation in vision-language systems. HANIA keeps both the vision-language model and the instruction-tuned decoder frozen, relying instead on graph construction, a two-group finite-state planner, and coverage-aware pruning to select evidence; evaluation on ScienceQA covers answer accuracy, evidence-filtering quality, evidence-budget sensitivity, and efficiency. The approach is an incremental academic contribution with no reported industry deployment, and its results are limited to a single benchmark.

Researchers propose FSCE, a Target-Aware Frequency-Spatial Collaborative Enhancement framework that combines a Frequency-Spatial Early-stage Adaptive Enhancement (FS-EAE) module with an Adaptive Policy-driven Semantic Alignment (APSA) mechanism to suppress speckle noise propagation in SAR ATR. The 0.17M-parameter variant suggests deployment potential on resource-constrained platforms. The FS-EAE module operates at the network entrance to stabilize shallow features via collaborative spatial-frequency modeling, while APSA uses an online teacher policy to impose top-down semantic constraints and feed guidance back to early features during training. The lightweight FSCE-Net_mu with 0.17M parameters demonstrates applicability to both high-capacity and lightweight architectures.

Researchers released TiAb Review Plugin, an open-source Chrome extension that provides no-code, serverless AI-assisted study selection for systematic reviews, covering both title/abstract (T&A) screening and full-text screening. It uses Google Sheets as a shared database, Google Drive as a PDF store, and user-supplied LLM API keys, and its re-implemented TypeScript ASReview classifier (TF-IDF with Naive Bayes) produced top-100 rankings identical to the Python original across six datasets. Systematic reviews are labor-intensive, and existing server-based screening tools charge subscription fees while open-source alternatives demand coding skills, so this extension lowers the barrier for reviewers without programming expertise. By covering full-text screening—previously outside the scope of no-code open-source tools—and reporting strong LLM recall, it could make AI-assisted evidence synthesis more accessible to small teams and resource-limited settings. For T&A screening the tool offers manual review, LLM batch screening, and ML active learning; for full-text screening it retrieves open-access PDFs from PubMed Central, Europe PMC, Unpaywall, OpenAlex, and publisher pages, supports blinded dual review with structured exclusion reasons and adjudication, and computes PRISMA 2020 flow counts. LLM T&A screening achieved 94%–100% recall with only 2%–15% precision and WSS@95 of 46.3%–89.3%, and the authors note that full-text classification accuracy has not yet been evaluated.

## [Adversarial Multi-task Learning for Joint Interference Detection and Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08607) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes AMTIDIN, an adversarial multi-task learning network that jointly performs interference detection, modulation identification, and interference identification in wireless communication systems. It derives an upper bound on the weighted expected loss that explicitly links multi-task learning performance to task similarity, quantified via the Wasserstein distance and learnable task relation coefficients. This work addresses a key gap in multi-task learning for wireless communications by providing a theoretical foundation for modeling task relationships, which could improve the robustness and survivability of communication systems in non-cooperative environments. The findings may influence how future deep learning models for spectrum sensing and interference management are designed, especially under limited data and low SNR conditions. AMTIDIN integrates adversarial training to minimize distributional discrepancies across tasks and uses adaptive coefficients to dynamically model task correlations. Experiments show it outperforms independently trained single-task counterparts and MTL baselines under limited training data, short signal lengths, and low SNRs, and a quantitative analysis reveals that modulation identification and interference identification share substantial feature overlap distinct from interference detection.

Adversarial learning involves training models to be robust against intentionally misleading inputs or to minimize distributional discrepancies between tasks.

## [IB-Flow Uses Information Bottleneck to Improve Few-Step Text-to-Image Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09133) ⭐️ 6.0/10

IB-Flow introduces an information bottleneck-guided framework for distilling classifier-free guidance (CFG) trajectories in few-step text-to-image diffusion models. It replaces the conventional static guidance strength and random supervisor timestep selection with a dual-track adaptive mechanism: an instance-aware selection based on local vector field norm and an entropy-aware schedule that decays with SNR, achieving state-of-the-art fidelity at 2-step generation. This work addresses a key inefficiency in few-step diffusion distillation, where static CFG injection causes over-conditioning artifacts and limits performance. By dynamically adapting guidance, it could enable faster, higher-quality text-to-image generation, benefiting real-time applications and resource-constrained deployments. The method formalizes distillation as a mutual information game under the Information Bottleneck principle, converting the intractable KL divergence constraint into a zero-overhead closed-form solution using the local vector field norm. It applies maximal guidance for initial structural anchoring and then decays it to refine micro-details, eradicating over-conditioning artifacts under extremely stringent 2-step configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Classifier-free guidance (CFG) is a standard technique in diffusion models that improves sample quality by combining conditional and unconditional predictions, but it requires multiple iterative steps, causing slow inference. Few-step distillation compresses the multi-step CFG trajectory into a student model that generates images in one or a few steps, but existing methods use a globally static guidance strength and randomly sample supervisor timesteps, ignoring the dynamic entropy reduction during generation. The Information Bottleneck principle from information theory provides a framework for finding the best tradeoff between compression and preserving relevant information, and has been applied to deep learning for improved generalization.

## [VERPO converts teacher guidance into token-level credit for policy optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06100) ⭐️ 6.0/10

VERPO introduces a method that decomposes evidence-conditioned teacher guidance into an evidence-free reference term and signed, evidence-induced corrections at each token, then uses a stopped controller to selectively accept and rescale those corrections. It also proposes Fisher Evidence Contrast (FEC), which attenuates nuisance shifts along an estimated evidence-presence direction via a regularized projection. Across five scientific reasoning and tool-use tasks, VERPO prevents optimization collapse and achieves the highest multi-task average across model backbones, with marked gains on smaller models. GRPO-style methods apply a single sequence-level advantage uniformly across all tokens, which cannot distinguish which local decisions should be preserved, reinforced, or revised. VERPO addresses this coarse credit assignment problem, making it relevant for researchers working on reinforcement learning for LLM alignment and reasoning, especially where verifiable rewards are available. The stopped controller balances alignment with the local GRPO update direction against Fisher movement cost, combining selective acceptance, token-wise localization, and cost-aware scaling. Qualitative diagnostics indicate that token acceptance selectively targets reasoning bottlenecks consistent with local reward alignment and Fisher movement cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm for training language models from rewards; for each prompt it samples a group of responses and uses the group's mean reward as a baseline for advantages, dropping PPO's critic. However, GRPO assigns the same sequence-level advantage to every token, so it cannot tell which local decisions within a response deserve credit. Evidence-conditioned self-distillation offers denser token-level supervision, but teacher imitation can transfer stylistic artifacts and miscalibrated confidence that destabilize training when misaligned with task success.

## [EvidenceNet: Verifying AI Agent Outcomes Across Network Authority Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10181) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.10181v2) introduces EvidenceNet, a runtime assurance layer that decides whether coordinated AI agent operations have actually achieved an operator's network-wide intent. Its broker collects post-change observations required by a completion contract, while an admission gate checks that evidence comes from the required authority scopes, remains current, and satisfies task rules, with a verifier agent assessing observation content. As AI agents take on more network automation tasks, the fragmentation of evidence across administrative domains makes it hard to confirm that a change actually propagated network-wide. EvidenceNet addresses this completion admission problem, which could improve trust and safety in multi-agent network operations where a successful action record alone is not proof of the intended outcome. Experiments on live routing networks show that post-change state checks recognize successful outcomes that configuration-action records alone cannot establish. Controlled interventions further demonstrate that EvidenceNet rejects completion when otherwise satisfactory observations come from the wrong source, have been substituted, or are stale.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Operational networks typically span many devices and administrative domains, and AI agents are given distinct authority scopes that define which resources they can access, which operations they can invoke, and which network state they can observe. This scoping limits the blast radius of an erroneous action but fragments the evidence needed to assess the network-wide outcome, and a valid observation can become stale after a subsequent change. EvidenceNet is positioned as a trusted assurance layer that collects current observations from the required scopes before declaring a coordinated operation complete.

<a id="item-296"></a>
## [RAIN: One-Step Region-Aware Extraction of Diffusion Model Watermarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14856) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose RAIN, a lightweight, prompt-free extractor that recovers semantic watermarks from diffusion models in a single step by decomposing endpoint recovery into an image-like anchor and a noise-oriented residual. The method avoids iterative diffusion inversion and repeated evaluation of a diffusion-scale U-Net, and its noise extraction cost is lower than both OSI and FARI. Semantic watermark extraction is central to provenance verification and copyright protection for AI-generated images, but multi-step inversion is computationally expensive. By cutting extraction to a single step with lower cost than prior one-step methods, RAIN could make watermark verification practical at scale for image generation services and content moderation pipelines. The key insight is that near the high-SNR image endpoint, recovering a useful noise statistic from the first-step output of extended flow matching is far simpler than reconstructing the full inverse trajectory, and Gaussian Shading only needs the recovered latent to fall within the correct watermark decision region. The decomposition into an image-like anchor and noise-oriented residual also improves GPU parallel utilization, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic watermarks embed ownership information directly into the initial noise latent of a diffusion model, so the watermark is inherently tied to the generated image's semantics. Gaussian Shading is a provable performance-lossless watermarking scheme for diffusion models, but extracting its watermark conventionally requires multi-step diffusion inversion to recover that initial noise. Flow matching is a generative modeling framework closely related to diffusion that transforms noise into data along Gaussian probability paths, and recent one-step methods such as OSI and FARI have shown the extraction cost can be substantially reduced.

<a id="item-297"></a>
## [Cross-Machine CNC Transfer of Latent World Models Yields Modest Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16071) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv study (2609.16071v2) tests whether a command-conditioned latent world model trained on a source CNC machine with 17 sensor channels can transfer to a target machine sharing only 10 of those channels. It reports two findings: latent-predictive pretraining provides no in-domain forecasting gain over matched training from scratch, and the transferred model beats a persistence baseline (R²≈0.01) but trails simple forecasters that normalize each input window by its own statistics. The results suggest that cross-machine transfer under partial sensor overlap is a distinct and difficult evaluation axis for command-conditioned world models, and that source-domain accuracy alone cannot reveal what a learned latent representation is worth. This matters for industrial AI practitioners who hope to reuse models across heterogeneous factory equipment without full sensor parity. The protocol is strict: all model selection uses source data only, the configuration is locked, and the target is evaluated exactly once, with a post-lock ablation declared before it ran. That ablation shows input normalization alone closes the gap to official forecasters, but doing so costs predictive calibration, and the overall target R² of roughly 0.01 against the target mean is very modest.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: A latent world model learns a compact internal representation of a system's state and dynamics, then predicts future representations rather than reconstructing raw future samples. Command-conditioned models take explicit high-level commands as input to modulate predictions, and persistence forecasting is a simple baseline that assumes future values equal the most recent observation. CNC machines are computer-controlled machining tools whose sensor channels and command conventions often differ across manufacturers, making transfer between them non-trivial.

<a id="item-298"></a>
## [LLM Valence Direction Reflects History, Not Just Outcome](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22850) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.22850v2) shows that a decodable good-bad outcome direction in LLMs transfers across different surface encodings of the same outcome, but transfer degrades substantially when the same realized outcome is reached through announced versus unannounced histories. Even after both histories receive the same explicit outcome, the post-event readout remains strongly conditioned on whether the outcome was previously announced. This matters for interpretability and AI welfare research because decodability and successful activation steering alone do not prove what an internal direction actually represents. The finding cautions against identifying such directions with a history-invariant scalar valence state, which is directly relevant to how researchers attribute welfare-relevant functional states to LLMs. The study uses a controlled maze task and multiple LLM checkpoints, and in a matched maze-RL run the post-RL direction becomes substantially more predictive of reference-MDP remaining return while the policy becomes more dependent on it at the tested sites. However, the history dependence persists even after RL, supporting a functional, value-related interpretation but not identification with a history-invariant scalar valence state.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to understand the internal workings of neural networks by analyzing their structures, algorithms, and circuits, and activation steering modifies internal activations at inference time to change model behavior. A central methodological question is whether a direction that can be decoded from activations, and that successfully steers behavior, represents a genuine functional state or merely a correlated feature of the extraction contrast. This question is especially consequential for AI welfare, where researchers try to determine whether AI systems could have morally significant internal states.

<a id="item-299"></a>
## [Jev Evaluated as Semantic Decision Component for Scientific Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24965) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.24965v2) evaluates Jev, TypeSafe's semantic decision model, using a harness that assigns arithmetic to code and follows documented guidance. Across twelve model configurations, twenty source-grounded Choices, ten scientific cases, and five repetitions each, Jev matched five other configurations at complete semantic correctness and achieved the lowest observed median latency among successful responses. The study shows that incorrect semantic choices can silently alter downstream counts while leaving final claim labels correct, meaning workflows may appear reproducible even when their underlying relations are wrong. This has direct implications for AI-for-science reproducibility, where reusing relations and quantities across analyses is common. Across three comparison models, seven wrong selections on a single culture-history question changed downstream counts while preserving the correct final label. The harness separates measurement of semantic selections, downstream outputs, and final claim labels, which is what makes this propagation visible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific workflows often require deciding among known relations—such as whether observations share a culture, treatment, or reference standard—before a deterministic calculation can proceed. Jev is TypeSafe's System One model, accessed via API or SDK, that reads text or JSON context called state and returns typed answers to predefined questions; it is designed as a machine-consumed decision component rather than a general-purpose LLM. This paper tests whether such a component can be trusted for the small semantic decisions that scientific pipelines depend on.

<a id="item-300"></a>
## [SpeakerMem-R1: Dual-Track Memory for Multi-Party Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26780) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SpeakerMem-R1 introduces a speaker-centered dual-track memory framework that stores speaker-labeled verbatim messages alongside derived person-level and group-level states, then combines both tracks by entity, event, and time at query time. It reports binary accuracies of 47.9%, 69.2%, and 61.9% on GroupMemBench, SocialMemBench, and EverMemBench, and 62.33% on the EverMemBench leaderboard, the best reported result among recent state-of-the-art frameworks. Multi-party dialogue memory is a genuine gap for LLM-based assistants, since general-purpose memory systems tend to lose person and group relations or fail to integrate clues spread across members, groups, and time. A speaker-centered dual-track design could improve message attribution and relational understanding in long-running group conversations, benefiting researchers and builders of conversational AI memory systems. The system trains a Writer-R1 component with SpeakerLevenshtein and speaker-conditioned GRPO to reduce attribution and update errors during structured memory construction while enabling local deployment; in a controlled evaluation of 305 questions, reinforcement learning raised the SFT Writer's mean accuracy from 57.38% to 68.20%. Ablations show the verbatim and structured tracks, as well as person-level and group-level views, are complementary, and the paper also reports 70.85% on all 1,986 LoCoMo questions as a two-person long-term conversation boundary test.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 24, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term conversational memory for LLMs usually means retrieving relevant past content, but multi-party settings add the harder problems of distinguishing who said what, whom each statement concerns, how individuals perceive one another, and what the group collectively shares. Existing benchmarks such as GroupMemBench, SocialMemBench, EverMemBench, and LoCoMo test these abilities, and prior general-purpose memory systems often struggle with them. SpeakerMem-R1 addresses this by separating verbatim, speaker-labeled messages from derived person and group states, an approach inspired by dual-process ideas of complementary fast and structured representations.
